\rk E_{s,u}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
for an open genus-one vertex
u\in V(\Delta)
and
\nu(s)\in T^{I}(u)
, then
T_{s-1,u}
vanishes nowhere on
K_{s,u}\subseteq{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
.

Remark 4.7
.

Note that, assuming
\bm{s}_{\Gamma}
is a
\gamma
-tame perturbation of TRR, for any
\Delta\in\partial^{*}\Gamma
, if
u\in V(\Delta)
is genus-one, then for any
0\leq j\leq D
the multisection
T_{j,u}
is a
\gamma|_{u,j}
-tame perturbation of TRR, where
\gamma|_{u,j}
is the order induced by
\gamma
on the set
\{i\in I_{\Gamma}^{d>0}\cap T^{I}(u)\colon i\leq_{\gamma}\nu(j)\}\subseteq I_{\Gamma}^{d>0}
.

Remark 4.8
.

For any open vertex
u
with
\rk E_{s,u}>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
,
T_{s,u}
vanishes nowhere on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
by transversality. Moreover, as a corollary of item
3
and
5
, if
\rk E_{s,u}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
for an open genus-one vertex
u\in V(\Gamma)
, then
T_{s,u}
also vanishes nowhere on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
.

Definition 4.9
.

For
g=0
or
1
, let
I,B
be the sets of internal and boundary markings such that
\lvert B\rvert+2\lvert I\rvert\geq 3-2g
, we consider the vector bundle

E:=\mathcal{W}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{g,B,I}.

Let
\gamma
be an order on the set
I^{d>0}:=\{i\in I\colon d_{i}\geq 1\}
.
For a multisection
\bm{s}
of
E
, we write
\bm{s}=s^{\mathcal{W}}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}\bigoplus_{1\leq t\leq d_{i}}s_{i}^{t},
where
s^{\mathcal{W}}
is a multisection of
\mathcal{W}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{g,B,I}
, and each
s_{i}^{t}
is a multisection of
\mathbb{L}_{i}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{g,B,I}
.
We call
s^{\mathcal{W}}
and
\bigoplus_{i\in I}\bigoplus_{1\leq t\leq d_{i}}s_{i}^{t}
the
\mathcal{W}
-part and
\mathbb{L}
-part of
\bm{s}
respectively.
We say the multisection
\bm{s}
is
\gamma
-canonical
if the following conditions hold.

1.

The multisection
\bm{s}
glues to a multisection of

\widetilde{E}:=\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{g,B,I},

i.e.,
the restrictions of
\bm{s}
to the type-AI boundaries of
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{g,B,I}
are identified with the restrictions of
\bm{s}
to the corresponding type-BI boundaries of
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{g,B,I}
under
\sim_{PI}
.

2.

We denote by
\bm{s}_{\bm{G}}
the restriction of
\bm{s}
to
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}\subseteq{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
for
\bm{G}\in\text{sGPI}^{r,0}_{1,B,I}
, we require that

\bm{s}_{\bm{G}}=\bboxplus_{\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})}\bm{s}_{\Gamma},

(4.3)

where
\bm{s}_{\Gamma}=s_{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I\cap T^{I}(\Gamma)}\bigoplus_{1\leq t\leq d_{i}}s_{i,\Gamma}^{t}
is a transverse multisection of the vector bundle

E_{\Gamma}:=\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I\cap T^{I}(\Gamma)}\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\Gamma\to\mathcal{B}\Gamma}\mathbb{L}_{i,\mathcal{B}\Gamma}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma}.

3.

In the case
\bm{G}
is genus-one (
i.e.
,
g=1
), for any
\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})
, we denote by
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma
the graph obtained by forgetting all the internal tails of
\Gamma
in
I^{PI}_{sp,1}(\mathbf{G})\cap T^{I}(\Gamma)\subset T^{I}(I)
.
Notice that all the tails in
I^{PI}_{sp,1}
have twist zero, therefore
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma
is still a
r
-spin graph, and it is always stable when
T(\Gamma)\setminus RT(\Gamma)\neq\emptyset
.
We denote by
\operatorname{For}_{\Gamma\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
the forgetful morphism. We require that
\bm{s}_{\Gamma}
is of the form

\bm{s}_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\Gamma\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}\bm{s}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}

(4.4)

under the natural identification between
\operatorname{For}_{\Gamma\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}E_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
and
E_{\Gamma}
(when
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma
unstable these bundles are rank-zero), where
\bm{s}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
is a transverse multisection of the vector bundle

E_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}:=\mathcal{W}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I\cap T^{I}(\Gamma)}\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\to\mathcal{B}\Gamma}\mathbb{L}_{i,\mathcal{B}\Gamma}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}.

4.

When
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma
is stable, we can decompose
\bm{s}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
with respect to the order by writing

\bm{s}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}=t_{0,\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}\oplus t_{1,\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}\oplus\dots\oplus t_{D,\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma},

where
t_{0,\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
is a multisection of
\mathcal{W}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
, and for
1\leq j\leq D
,
t_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
is a multisection of
\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\to{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}\mathbb{L}_{\nu(j),{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
which pulls back to
t_{j}
if
\nu(j)\in T^{I}(\Gamma)
, and is just a formal section of rank-zero bundle otherwise.
Then, for any
\Delta\in\partial^{*}\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma
and any
0\leq s\leq D
, the multisection

\bigoplus_{j=0}^{s}t_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}

is transversely decomposable at
\Delta
.

5.

If
\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})
is genus-zero, then the
\mathcal{W}
-part
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Gamma}
of section
\bm{s}_{\Gamma}
, as a multisection of
\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma}
, is positive (see Definition
2.24
).

6.

If
\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})
is genus-one and
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma
is stable, then the multisection
\bm{s}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
is a
\gamma|_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
-tame perturbation of TRR section, where
\gamma|_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
is the order on
I^{d>0}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}=T^{I}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma)\cap I^{d>0}\subseteq I^{d>0}
induced by
\gamma
.
Note that in this case we have
{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}={{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
.

Proposition 4.10
.

With the above notations, for
g=0
or
1
, for any order
\gamma
on
I^{d>0}
, there exist at least one
\gamma
-canonical multisection of
E\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{g,B,I}^{1/r}
.

We postpone the construction of
\gamma
-canonical multisections in the
g=1
case to §
6
. A construction in the
g=0
case can be found in Proposition
4.16
.

Observation 4.11
.

Let
\bm{s}
be a
\gamma
-canonical multisection of
E
in the case
\rk E=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{g,B,I}^{1/r}
, then
\bm{s}
vanishes nowhere on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
for
\bm{G}\in\text{sGPI}^{r,0}_{g,B,I}
unless for any
\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})
we have

\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma=\Gamma
and
\rk E_{\Gamma}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}
. This follows from the transversality of
\bm{s}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
, and the equations

\sum_{\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})}\rk E_{\Gamma}=\rk E,\quad\sum_{\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})}\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,B,I}^{1/r},

\rk E_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}=\rk E_{\Gamma},\text{ and }\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma}\geq\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}.

In particular, as a result of Remark
4.8
and item
6
in Definition
4.9
,
\bm{s}
vanishes nowhere on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
as long as there exist a genus-one
\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})
.

Observation 4.12
.

Let
\bm{s}
be a
\gamma
-canonical multisection of
E
in the case
\rk E=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{g,B,I}^{1/r}
, then for any
\bm{G}\in\text{sGPI}^{r,0}_{g,B,I}
, there exists an open neighbourhood
U_{\bm{G}}\subseteq{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\bm{G}}
of

\partial^{R}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\bm{G}}\cup\partial^{NS+}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\bm{G}}\cup\mathcal{Z}^{dj}_{\bm{G}}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\bm{G}},

such that
\bm{s}
vanishes nowhere on

(U_{\bm{G}}\cup\partial^{CB}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\bm{G}})\cap{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}.

In fact, when there exist genus-one
\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})
, we can take
U_{\bm{G}}={\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\bm{G}}
according to Observation
4.11
. When all
\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})
are genus-zero, then
\mathcal{Z}^{dj}_{\bm{G}}=\emptyset
and the existence of
U_{\bm{G}}
follows from the positivity constraint for the
\mathcal{W}
-part on genus-zero components (
i.e.
item
5
in Definition
4.9
). Note that
\bm{s}
also nowhere vanishes on

(\partial^{BI}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\bm{G}}\cup\partial^{AI}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\bm{G}})\cap{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}

as consequence of item
4
in Definition
4.9
.

Because
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}\setminus U_{\bm{G}}
is compact, we can define (see
[
4
, Appendix A]
) the Euler number

\int_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}}e(E;\mathbf{s})\in\mathbb{Q}

with respect to the multisection
\bm{s}
as the zero count (with multiplicity)
\#Z(\bm{s})
of
\bm{s}
in
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
for each
\bm{G}\in\text{sGPI}^{r,0}_{1,B,I}
, using the canonical relative orientation of
E
over
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
constructed in
[
29
]
.

Definition 4.13
.

With the above notations, for
g=0
or
1
, if
\text{rank}E=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{g,B,I}^{1/r}
, we refer to the integral

\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma,\mathbf{s}}_{g}:=\int_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{g,B,I}}e(E;\mathbf{s})\in\mathbb{Q},

where
\mathbf{s}
is a
\gamma
-canonical multisection,
as an
open genus-
g

r
-spin intersection number with respect to
\gamma
and
\bm{s}
. For shortness we sometimes call the above number an
ordered open genus-one
r
-spin intersection number
, or a
correlator
. When
\text{rank}(E)\neq\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{g,B,I}^{1/r}
, the integral is defined to be zero.

Remark 4.14
.

If
d_{i}=0
for all
i\in I
, then the genus-one correlators are always zero because in this case by (
2.15
) we have

\rk E=\rk\mathcal{W}_{1,B,I}<\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{1,B,I}^{1/r}.

4.2
Genus-zero case

In this subsection, we will show the above correlators are independent of the choice of the order
\gamma
and multisection
\mathbf{s}
in
g=0
case. We will also compare them with the genus-zero open
r
-spin correlators defined in
[
4
]
. The proof of independence of choice in the
g=1
case is postponed to §
5.2
.

In the
g=0
case, the
\gamma
-canonicity in Definition
4.9
is in fact independent of the order
\gamma
. We simply refer to the
\gamma
-canonical multisections as
canonical multisections
in
g=0
case.

Proposition 4.15
.

For any two canonical multisections
\mathbf{s}^{a}
and
\mathbf{s}^{b}
of

E:=\mathcal{W}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I},

we have

\int_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{0,B,I}}e(E;\mathbf{s}^{a})=\int_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{0,B,I}}e(E;\mathbf{s}^{b}),

i.e., the genus-zero open
r
-spin correlators are independent of the choice of the order
\gamma
and canonical multisection
\bm{s}
, and we can write

\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}:=\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma,\mathbf{s}}_{0}

for any such choice.

Proof.

This is almost a special case (
{\mathfrak{h}}=0
) of
[
30
, Theorem 3.9]
. Although in
[
30
]
the relative cotangent line bundles are
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}_{i}
, the proof is identical. We outline the key ideas of the proof.

We regard
\mathbf{s}^{a}
and
\mathbf{s}^{b}
as multisections over
\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}
. Note that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}
is a compact space with boundaries of type CB, NS+ or R, while
\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}
only has boundaries of type CB. We write

\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}=\partial^{CB}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}\cup\partial^{+}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I},

where
\partial^{+}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}
is the union of all type-NS+ and type-R boundaries.

Since both
\mathbf{s}^{a}
and
\mathbf{s}^{b}
are canonical, for any
0\leq t\leq 1
, the multisection
(1-t)\mathbf{s}^{a}+t\mathbf{s}^{b}
does not vanish at any point
p\in\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}\cap\partial^{CB}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}
. In fact, assuming
p\in{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma^{CB}}\times\prod_{\Gamma\in V(\mathbf{G})\setminus\{\Gamma^{CB}\}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma}
where
\Gamma^{CB}
has a contracted boundary tail, then both
\mathbf{s}^{a}_{\Gamma^{CB}}
,
\mathbf{s}^{b}_{\Gamma^{CB}}
and
(1-t)\mathbf{s}^{a}_{\Gamma^{CB}}+t\mathbf{s}^{b}_{\Gamma^{CB}}

evaluate positively at the corresponding contracted boundary node. For similar reason, there exists an open neighbourhood
U^{+}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}
of
\partial^{+}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}
, such that for any
0\leq t\leq 1
, the multisection
(1-t)\mathbf{s}^{a}+t\mathbf{s}^{b}
does not vanish at any point
p\in\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}\cap U^{+}
.

Therefore,
(1-t)\mathbf{s}^{a}+t\mathbf{s}^{b}
is a homotopy between
\mathbf{s}^{a}
and
\mathbf{s}^{a}
which vanishes nowhere on
\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}\cap(U^{+}\cup\partial^{CB}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I})
, we can conclude the proof using
[
4
, Lemma 4.12]
.

∎

In
[
6
,
4
]
, an genus-zero open
r
-spin theory, to which we refer as the BCT theory, was constructed in an different way. For a graded
r
-spin graph
\Gamma,
we denote by
\mathcal{F}\Gamma
the graph obtained by detaching all the NS edges of
\Gamma
whose twist at the illegal half-edge is
0
, then forgetting all the twist-
0
illegal half-edges. Note that forgetting tails may produce unstable components
\mathcal{F}\Gamma
; we view the moduli space corresponding to an unstable component, which is a direct summand of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathcal{F}\Gamma}
, as a single point. Let
F_{\Gamma}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathcal{F}\Gamma}
be the induced map in the level of moduli spaces.

[
4
, Proposition 6.2]
constructed the
special BCT-canonical multisections
, which are collections of transverse multisections
\hat{s}^{\text{BCT}}_{\Gamma}
of

E_{\Gamma}:=\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in T^{I}(\Gamma)\cap I}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma}

for every graded
r
-spin graph
\Gamma
, which satisfy, in addition to the same positivity as in Definition
2.24
, that for every
\Gamma\in\partial^{*}\Delta

\hat{s}^{\text{BCT}}_{\Delta}\big|_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}}=F_{\Gamma}^{*}\hat{s}^{\text{BCT}}_{\mathcal{F}\Gamma}.

(4.5)

Note that the moduli space
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r}_{0,B,I}
is the disjoint union

{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r}_{0,B,I}=\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Gamma\text{ smooth}\\
T^{I}(\Gamma)=I,T^{B}(\Gamma)=B\end{subarray}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma},

the collection of the multisections
\hat{s}^{\text{BCT}}_{\Gamma}
for such
\Gamma
is a multisection
\hat{s}_{0,B,I}^{BCT}
of the vector bundle

\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in T^{I}(\Gamma)\cap I}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r}_{0,B,I}.

The BCT-correlators are the number of zero of such BCT-canonical multisections,
i.e.,

\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,BCT}_{0}=\int_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r}_{0,B,I}}e(E;\hat{s}_{0,B,I}^{BCT})\in\mathbb{Q}.

Proposition 4.16
.

There exists at least one canonical multisection of

E=\mathcal{W}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}.

Moreover, we have

\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}=\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,BCT}_{0}.

(4.6)

Proof.

Note that
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r}_{0,B,I}
can be regard as a subspace of
\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}
, similar to the proof of
[
30
, Theorem 5.2]
, the strategy is to extend to multisection
\mathbf{s}^{BCT}
over
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r}_{0,B,I}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r}_{0,B,I}
to a canonical multisection over the entire
\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}
which vanishes nowhere on
\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}\setminus{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r}_{0,B,I}
.

For any
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}
, recall that for
\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})
we can forget all inserted (twist-zero) internal tails in
T^{I}(\Gamma)\cap I^{PI}(\bm{G})
and obtain a graph
\mathcal{B}\Gamma
. We construct
\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{G}}
as

\hat{\mathbf{s}}_{\mathbf{G}}:=\bboxplus_{\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})}\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\Gamma\to\mathcal{B}\Gamma}\hat{s}^{\text{BCT}}_{\mathcal{B}\Gamma}.

which is in fact a multisection of
\mathcal{W}\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{l}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
because we are working with
\mathbb{L}_{i}
instead of
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}_{i}
(see §
2.4.3
).

The collection
\hat{\mathbf{s}}_{\mathbf{G}}
is canonical multisection in Definition
4.9
. In fact, item
1
is a consequence of (
4.5
), item
2
follows from the construction, item
5
is the property of the BCT-canonical multisections
\hat{s}^{BCT}_{\Gamma}
, while item
3
and
6
are irrelevant in the
g=0
case.

Over
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r}_{0,B,I}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B,I}
,
i.e.,
the union of
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}={\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{\bm{G}}}
for single-vertex
\bm{G}
with
V(\bm{G})=\{\Gamma_{\bm{G}}\}
, the multisection
\hat{\bm{s}}_{\mathbf{G}}
coincides with
\hat{s}^{BCT}_{\Gamma_{\bm{G}}}=\hat{s}_{0,B,I}^{BCT}|_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}}
by definition.

Over
\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r}_{0,B,I}\setminus{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r}_{0,B,I}
,
i.e.,
the union of
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
for
(r,0)
-graphs
\bm{G}
with more than one vertex; we show that
\hat{\bm{s}}_{\bm{G}}
never vanishes for such
\bm{G}
. In fact, we have

\sum_{\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})}\rk E_{\mathcal{B}\Gamma}=\sum_{\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})}\rk E_{\Gamma}=\sum_{\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})}\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma}>\sum_{\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})}\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{B}\Gamma}

because at least one inserted internal tail is forgotten, therefore
\rk E_{\mathcal{B}\Gamma}>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{B}\Gamma}
for at least one
\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})
, then by transversality
\hat{s}^{\text{BCT}}_{\mathcal{B}\Gamma}
vanishes nowhere, hence
\hat{\bm{s}}_{\bm{G}}
vanishes nowhere. This proves (
4.6
).

∎

As a consequence, these genus-zero open
r
-spin correlators satisfy the same topological recursion relations as in
[
4
]
.

Theorem 4.17

(
[
4
, Theorem 4.1]
)
.

The genus-zero open
r
-spin correlators satisfy the following two types of Topological Recursion Relations (note that all boundary twists
b_{i}
are
r-2
):

(a)

Suppose
\lvert I\rvert,\lvert B\rvert\geq 1
, then for any
i_{1}\in I
and
j_{B}\in B
, we have the following TRR with respect to
(i_{1},j_{B})
:

\begin{split}&\left\langle\tau_{d_{i_{1}}+1}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}\hskip-5.69046pt\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{{i_{1}}\}\\
-1\leq a\leq r-2\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{a}\tau_{d_{i_{1}}}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau_{d_{i}}^{a_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},\text{ext}}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{r-2-a}\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}\\
&+\hskip-2.84544pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{{i_{1}}\}\\
T_{1}\sqcup T_{2}=B\setminus\{j_{B}\}\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau^{a_{i_{1}}}_{d_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in T_{1}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\sigma^{r-2}\sigma^{b_{j_{B}}}\prod_{i\in T_{2}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}.\end{split}

(4.7)

(b)

Suppose
\lvert I\rvert\geq 2
, then for any
i_{1}\in I
and
j_{I}\in I\setminus\{i_{1}\}
, we have the following TRR with respect to
(i_{1},j_{I})
:

\begin{split}&\left\langle\tau_{d_{i_{1}}+1}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}\hskip-5.69046pt\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{i_{1},j_{I}\}\\
-1\leq a\leq r-2\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{a}\tau_{d_{i_{1}}}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau_{d_{i}}^{a_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},\text{ext}}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{r-2-a}\tau^{a_{j_{I}}}_{d_{j_{I}}}\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}\\
&+\hskip-2.84544pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{i_{1},j_{I}\}\\
T_{1}\sqcup T_{2}=B\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau^{a_{i_{1}}}_{d_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in T_{1}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau^{a_{j_{I}}}_{d_{j_{I}}}\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\sigma^{r-2}\prod_{i\in T_{2}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}.\end{split}

(4.8)

Remark 4.18
.

A slightly different version of genus-zero open
r
-spin theory is constructed in
[
30
]
. The
{\mathfrak{h}}=0
theory in
[
30
]
is very similar to the
g=1
theory in this paper; the only difference is that we take different relative cotangent line bundles: in
[
30
]
the theory is constructed using
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}_{i}
(see Remark
2.34
), while in this paper we use
\mathbb{L}_{i}
forgetting all the inserted points. This difference results in a polynomial relation between those correlators, see
[
30
, Theorem 5.6, Remark 5.7]
.

5
Genus-one Topological Recursion Relation

In this section, we show that the genus-one open
r
-spin correlators also satisfy topological recursion relations, an prove that the genus-one open
r
-spin correlators are independent of the choice of the order
\gamma
and the canonical multisection
\bm{s}
as a corollary. We will also prove Theorems
1.5
and
1.6
using topological recursion relations.

5.1
Genus-one TRR for correlators respect to orders and multisections

Let
\gamma
be an order on the set
I^{d>0}=\{i\in I\colon d_{i}\geq 1\}
. We denote the maximal element in the order
\gamma
by
i_{1}=\gamma(I)
. For any subset
J\subseteq I
, we denote by
\gamma|_{J}
the order on
\{i\in J\colon d_{i}\geq 1\}
induced by
\gamma
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
i_{1}=\gamma(I)
be the maximal element in
I^{d>0}
with respect to the order
\gamma
and
\mathbf{s}
be a
\gamma
-canonical multisection, we have

\begin{split}&\left\langle\tau_{d_{i_{1}}+1}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma,\bm{s}}_{1}\hskip-5.69046pt\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{i_{1}\}\\
-1\leq a\leq r-2\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{a}\tau_{d_{i_{1}}}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau_{d_{i}}^{a_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},\text{ext}}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{r-2-a}\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma|_{R_{2}},\bm{s}|_{1,B,R_{2}\sqcup\{i^{r-2-a}_{I}\}}}_{1}\\
&+\hskip-2.84544pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{i_{1}\}\\
T_{1}\sqcup T_{2}=B\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau^{a_{i_{1}}}_{d_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in T_{1}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\sigma^{r-2}\prod_{i\in T_{2}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma|_{R_{2}},\bm{s}|_{1,T_{2}\sqcup\{i^{r-2}_{B}\},R_{2}}}_{1}\\
&+{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\sigma^{r-2}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0},\end{split}

(5.1)

where the notation
\bm{s}|_{1,I^{\prime},B^{\prime}}
in the superscripts of the genus-one correlators refers to
\gamma|_{I^{\prime}}
-canonical multisections (determined by
\bm{s}
) of the vector bundle

\mathcal{W}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I\cap I^{\prime}}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B^{\prime},I^{\prime}},

the notation
i^{r-2-a}_{I}
represents an internal markings with twist
r-2-a
, and
i^{r-2}_{B}
represents a boundary markings with twist
r-2
.

Proof.

Let

\bm{s}=s^{\mathcal{W}}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\bigoplus_{1\leq t\leq d_{i}}s_{i}^{t}\oplus\bigoplus_{1\leq t\leq d_{i_{1}}+1}s_{i_{1}}^{t}

be a
\gamma
-canonical multisection of

E_{1,B,I}:=\mathcal{W}_{1,B,I}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}\oplus\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}^{\oplus(d_{i_{1}}+1)}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}.

Under the similar notation as in §
4
we write

\bm{s}=t_{0}\oplus t_{1}\oplus\dots\oplus t_{D},

where
t_{D}
is a multisection of
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}
for the maximal element
i_{1}\in I^{d>0}
, and we write

\bm{s}^{re}:=t_{0}\oplus t_{1}\oplus\dots\oplus t_{D-1}

(5.2)

as a multisection of

E^{re}_{1,B,I}=\mathcal{W}_{1,B,I}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}.

For a
(r,0)
-graph
\bm{G}\in\text{sGPI}^{r,0}_{1,B,I}
, we denote by
\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\in V(\bm{G})
the unique vertex of
\bm{G}
such that
i_{1}=\nu(D)\in T^{I}(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})
. Since
\bm{s}=\bm{s}^{re}\oplus t_{D}
is
\gamma
-canonical, for any
\bm{G}\in\text{sGPI}^{r,0}_{1,B,I}
, we have a decomposition

\bm{s}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}=\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\oplus t_{D,\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}

for a multisection
t_{D,\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
of
\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
and a multisection
\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
of

E^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}:=\mathcal{W}_{{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I\cap T^{I}(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})}\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}^{d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}},

such that

\bm{s}^{re}_{\bm{G}}=\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\boxplus\bboxplus_{\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})\setminus\{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\}}\bm{s}_{\Gamma}.

We have a similar decomposition for the bundles and multisections on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
,
i.e,

E_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}=E^{re}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\oplus\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}

and

\bm{s}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}=\bm{s}^{re}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\oplus t_{D,\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}.

This proof is split into five steps:

1.

Construct a specific multisection
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
of
\mathbb{L}^{*}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
.

2.

Construct a multisection
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}
of
\mathbb{L}^{\vee}_{i_{1}}\otimes\mathbb{L}^{*}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
.

3.

Modify the multisection
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}\otimes{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
with ‘poles’ to a multisection
{s}^{trr}_{i_{1}}
of
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
without ‘poles’.

4.

Show that
\bm{s}
has the same number of zeros (with multiplicity) as
\bm{s}^{mod}:=\bm{s}^{re}\oplus{s}^{trr}_{i_{1}}
in
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
.

5.

Count the number of zeros (with multiplicity) of the restriction of
\bm{s}^{re}
to the zero locus of the multisection
{s}^{trr}_{i_{1}}
.

Step 1.

We first construct a specific multisection of
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
of
\mathbb{L}^{*}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
.

We need to construct a multisection
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}
of
{\mathbb{L}}^{*}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
for each smooth
\bm{G}
. Recall that in §
2.4.3
, the line bundle
{\mathbb{L}}^{*}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
is pulled back from
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{QM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
. Since
\bm{G}
is genus-one, the set

RT(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}):=T(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\cap\left(I^{PI}_{sp,0}(\bm{G})\sqcup B^{PI}_{sp,0}(\bm{G})\sqcup I^{PI}_{nsp}(\bm{G})\sqcup B^{PI}_{nsp}(\bm{G})\right)

is always empty for genus-one
\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}
, and is either a single-element set (where
RT(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\subseteq T(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\cap\left(I^{PI}_{sp,0}(\bm{G})\sqcup B^{PI}_{sp,0}(\bm{G})\right)
) or a two-element set (where
RT(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})=T(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\cap\left(I^{PI}_{nsp}(\bm{G})\sqcup B^{PI}_{nsp}(\bm{G})\right)
) for genus-zero
\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}
.

In the case
RT(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})
is empty,
i.e.,

\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
is genus-one, we have
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}={\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
. We take the restriction
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
of
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
to
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
to be the multisection pulled back from the TRR multisection

\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
of
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}={\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
via the projection
\pi_{\mathbf{G}\to\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}}
and the forgetful morphism
\operatorname{For}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
forgetting all the inserted tails.

In the case
RT(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})
is a single-element set, we denote by
r_{0}\in RT(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})
its unique element and take the section
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}
of
{\mathbb{L}}^{*}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
to be the section pulled back from the TRR section
\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},r_{0}}
of
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},r_{0}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
via the projection
\pi_{\mathbf{G}\to\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}}
and the forgetful morphism
\operatorname{For}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
forgetting the inserted tails in
I^{PI}_{sp,0}
.

In the case
RT(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})
is a two-element set, we denote by
r_{1},r_{2}\in RT(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})
its elements and take the multisection
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}
of
{\mathbb{L}}^{*}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
to be the multisection pulled back from the TRR multisection
\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},r_{1},r_{2}}
of
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},r_{1},r_{2}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
via the projection
\pi_{\mathbf{G}\to\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}}
and the morphism
\operatorname{For}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
forgetting the inserted tails in
I^{PI}_{sp,0}
.

The multisections
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}
defined above on each
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G}}
can be glued in the a multisection
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
of the glued line bundle
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{*}_{i_{1}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
. This follows from the behaviour of the multisections
\mathfrak{s}
at the boundary strata,
i.e.
(
3.3
), (
3.3
) and (
3.5
).

Following from the above construction and Observation
3.4
, the zero locus
Z({s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}})
of the multisection
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
of
{\mathbb{L}}^{*}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
(or
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{*}_{i_{1}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
after gluing) is of the form

Z({s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}})=\bigcup_{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},g=1}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}\cup\bigcup_{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},sp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}\bigcup_{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}\bigcup_{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2+1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}},

(5.3)

where

\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1}}:=\left\{\bm{G}\in{\text{GPI}}_{1,B,I}^{r,0}\colon\begin{array}[]{ccc}\text{For $\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\in V(\bm{G})$ such that $i_{1}\in T^{I}(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})$, $\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}$ consists of one closed}\\
\text{vertex $v_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},c}$ and one open vertex $v_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},c}$ connected by an internal}\\
\text{edge $e_{\bm{G}}$, where $\hat{\mathcal{B}}v_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},c}$ is stable, and all $\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})\setminus\{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\}$ are smooth}\end{array}\right\},

\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},g=1}:=\left\{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1}}\colon\begin{array}[]{c}\text{$\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}$ is genus-one}\end{array}\right\},

\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},sp}:=\left\{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1}}\colon\begin{array}[]{c}\text{$\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}$ is genus-zero, $\lvert RT(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\rvert=1$, $RT(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\subset T(v_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},o})$}\end{array}\right\},

\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}:=\left\{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1}}\colon\begin{array}[]{c}\text{$\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}$ is genus-zero, $\lvert RT(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\rvert=2$, $\lvert RT(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\cap T(v_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},o})\rvert=1$}\end{array}\right\},

\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2+1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}:=\left\{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1}}\colon\begin{array}[]{c}\text{$\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}$ is genus-zero, $\lvert RT(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\rvert=2$, $\lvert RT(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\cap T(v_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},o})\rvert=2$}\end{array}\right\}.

Remark 5.2
.

Note that
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
is contained in the zero locus of
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
over
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{d_{e}\bm{G}}
for
\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1}}
.
When
\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}\sqcup\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2+1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}
, the multisection
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
has two branches of weight
1/2
over
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{d_{e}\bm{G}}
; for
\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}
, only one branch out of two vanishes on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
; for
\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2+1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}
, both branch vanish on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
.

A subset of
\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},co}\subseteq\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},g=1}\sqcup\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},sp}\sqcup\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}\sqcup\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2+1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}
is particularly important in this proof, which is given by

\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},co}:=\left\{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},g=1}\sqcup\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},sp}\sqcup\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}\sqcup\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2+1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}\colon T(v_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},c})\cap I^{PI}(\bm{G})=\emptyset\right\}.

For each
\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1}}
, let
n_{\bm{G}}\in T(v_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},c})
be the unique internal half-edge, then by definition
T(v_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},c})\setminus\{n_{\bm{G}}\}
is always a non-empty subset of
I
containing
i_{1}
for
\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},co}
, thus we have a decomposition

\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},co}=\bigsqcup_{R\subseteq I\setminus\{i_{1}\},R\neq\emptyset}\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{R}_{i_{1},co},

where

\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{R}_{i_{1},co}:=\left\{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},co}\colon T(v_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},c})=I\sqcup\{i_{1},n_{\bm{G}}\}\right\}.

Step 2.

We will modify
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}
to a multisection
{s}^{trr}_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}
of
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
by tensoring with a specific section of the line bundle
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}^{*\vee}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
.
We first look at the dual line bundle
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}^{*}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}^{\vee}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
. When we restrict it to
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G}}\subseteq{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
, by definition it is pulled back from the line bundle

\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\otimes\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}^{\vee}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}

via
\pi_{\mathbf{G}\to\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}}

and
\operatorname{For}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
.
Notice that
{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
can be obtained by forgetting all the internal tails of
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
in
T^{I}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\cap RT(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})
, we have a section (see §
3.1
)
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
of the line bundle
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\otimes\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}^{\vee}
. We pullback
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
to a section
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*},\mathbf{G}}
of
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}^{*}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}^{\vee}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G}}
,
i.e.,

\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*},\mathbf{G}}:=\pi^{*}_{\mathbf{G}\to\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}}\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}.

These sections
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*},\mathbf{G}}
glue to a section
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}
of
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{*}_{i_{1}}\otimes\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{i_{1}}^{\vee}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
. In fact, assuming
\mathbf{G_{1}}
and
\mathbf{G_{2}}
are smooth
(r,0)
-graphs such that
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G_{1}}}
and
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G_{2}}}
are glued along their identified codimensional-1 boundaries
\overline{\text{bd}}_{BI}
and
\overline{\text{bd}}_{AI}
, then we have

\overline{\text{bd}}_{BI}={\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{1}^{BI}}\times\prod_{\Gamma\in V(\mathbf{G_{1}})\setminus\{\Gamma_{1}\}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma}

for a vertex
\Gamma_{1}\in V(\mathbf{G_{1}})
and
\Gamma^{BI}_{1}\in\partial\Gamma_{1}
with a unique (boundary) edge
e^{PI}
. We assume
e^{PI}
is separating (the case where
e^{PI}
is non-separating is similar), and we denote by
v_{0}
and
v_{r-2}
the two vertices of
\Gamma_{1}^{BI}
, where the half-edge of
e^{PI}
on
v_{0}
(respectively
v_{r-2}
) is illegal with twist
0
(respective legal with twist
r-2
). In this case we can write

\overline{\text{bd}}_{AI}={\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{2}^{AI}}\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v_{r-2}}\times\prod_{\Gamma\in V(\mathbf{G_{1}})\setminus\{\Gamma_{1}\}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma},

where
\Gamma_{2}^{AI}\in\partial\Gamma_{2}
(for
\Gamma_{2}\in V(\bm{G}_{2})=V(\bm{G}_{1})\sqcup\{\Gamma_{2},v_{r-2}\}\setminus\{\Gamma_{1}\}
) is an
r
-spin graph with two vertices, one of them is the same as
v_{0}
, the other vertex
v^{\prime}
has only one (inserted) internal tail and one boundary half-edge connecting to the twist-zero half-edge of
v_{0}
.

-

If
i_{1}\in T^{I}(\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G_{1}}})
for a
\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G_{1}}}\in V(\mathbf{G}_{1})\setminus{\Gamma_{1}}
, then
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*},\mathbf{G_{1}}}
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*},\mathbf{G_{2}}}
coincide on the identified boundary since both of them are pulled back from

\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G_{1}}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G_{1}}}}=\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G_{2}}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G_{2}}}}

over
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G_{1}}}}={\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G_{2}}}}
.

-

If
i_{1}\in T^{I}(v_{r-2})
, then
\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G_{1}}}=\Gamma_{1}
. By definition the restriction of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*},\mathbf{G_{1}}}
to
\overline{\text{bd}}_{AI}
is pulled back from

\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}}

via the projection
\pi_{\mathbf{G_{1}}\to\Gamma_{1}^{BI}}\colon\overline{\text{bd}}_{BI}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{1}^{BI}}
and
\operatorname{For}_{\Gamma_{1}^{BI}\to\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{1}^{BI}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}}
. The section

\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}}

itself is pulled back from
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}v_{r-2}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{r-2}}
via the projection
\pi_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}v_{r-2}}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}}\cong{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}v_{0}}\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}v_{r-2}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}v_{r-2}}
by the construction in §
3.1
, under the natural identifications between
\pi^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}v_{r-2}}\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}v_{r-2}}
and
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}}
, and between

\pi^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}v_{r-2}}\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}v_{r-2}\to\mathcal{B}v_{r-2}}\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\mathcal{B}v_{r-2}}=\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}\to\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}}\pi^{*}_{\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}\to\mathcal{B}v_{r-2}}\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\mathcal{B}v_{r-2}}

and
\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}\to\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}}\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{1}^{BI}}
.

On the other hand, the restriction of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*},\mathbf{G_{2}}}
to
\overline{\text{bd}}_{BI}
is also pulled back from
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}v_{r-2}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}v_{r-2}}
via the same map (after identify
\overline{\text{bd}}_{BI}
and
\overline{\text{bd}}_{AI}
) by definition, thus it coincides with
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*},\mathbf{G_{1}}}
on this identified boundary.

-

If
i_{1}\in T^{I}(v_{0})
, similar to the previous item, the restriction of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*},\mathbf{G_{1}}}
to
\overline{\text{bd}}_{BI}
is pulled back from
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}v_{0}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}v_{0}}
,
while the restriction of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*},\mathbf{G_{2}}}
to
\overline{\text{bd}}_{AI}
is also pulled back
from
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}v_{0}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}v_{0}}
via the same morphism after identifying
\overline{\text{bd}}_{BI}
and
\overline{\text{bd}}_{BI}
.

According to Lemma
3.2
and the construction above, the zero
Z(\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}})
locus of the section
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}
of
{\mathbb{L}}^{*}_{i_{1}}\otimes{\mathbb{L}}_{i_{1}}^{\vee}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
(or
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{*}_{i_{1}}\otimes\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{i_{1}}^{\vee}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
after gluing) is

Z(\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}})=\bigcup_{\mathbf{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{F}}_{i_{1},sp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G}}\cup\bigcup_{\mathbf{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{1}_{i_{1},nsp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G}}\cup\bigcup_{\mathbf{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{2}_{i_{1},nsp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G}},

where, in the notations in Step 1,

\bm{\mathcal{F}}_{i_{1},sp}:=\left\{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1}}\colon\text{ $RT(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})=\{i^{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}_{sp}\}\subseteq I^{PI}_{sp,0}(\bm{G})$, $T(v_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},c})=\{i_{1},n_{\bm{G}},i^{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}_{sp}\}$}\right\},

\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{1}_{i_{1},nsp}:=\left\{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1}}\colon\text{$\lvert RT(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\rvert=2$, $T(v_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},c})=\{i_{1},n_{\bm{G}},i^{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}_{nsp}\}$ for $i^{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}_{nsp}\in I^{PI}_{nsp}(\bm{G})$}\right\}

and

\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{2}_{i_{1},nsp}:=\left\{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1}}\colon\text{$\lvert RT(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\rvert=2$, $RT(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\subseteq I^{PI}_{nsp}(\bm{G})$, $T(v_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}},c})=\{i_{1},n_{\bm{G}}\}\sqcup RT(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})$}\right\}.

Note that for each
a\in I^{PI}_{sp,0}({\bm{G}})
, it induces partitions of
I=I_{a,0}\sqcup I_{a,1}
and
B=B_{a,0}\sqcup B_{a,1}
in the following way: when we remove the edge of
\hat{\bm{G}}
(see Definition
2.27
) corresponding to the dashed line between
a
and
\delta(a)
, we get two connected components
\hat{\bm{G}}_{1}
and
\hat{\bm{G}}_{0}
, where
a
is on the
\hat{\bm{G}}_{0}
side. We define

I_{s}(a):=I\cap\sqcup_{\Gamma\in V(\hat{\bm{G}}_{s})}T^{I}(\Gamma)
and
B_{s}(a):=B\cap\sqcup_{\Gamma\in V(\hat{\bm{G}}_{s})}T^{B}(\Gamma)
for
s=0,1
. Such partitions induces a decomposition

\bm{\mathcal{F}}_{i_{1},sp}=\bigsqcup_{R\subseteq I\setminus\{i_{1}\},T\subseteq B}\bm{\mathcal{F}}_{i_{1},sp}^{R,T},

where

\bm{\mathcal{F}}_{i_{1},sp}^{R,T}:=\{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{F}}_{i_{1},sp}\colon I_{0}(i_{sp}^{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}})=R\sqcup\{i_{1}\},B_{0}(i_{sp}^{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}})=T\}.

We consider the dual section
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}
of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}
, which is a section of
{\mathbb{L}}^{*\vee}_{i_{1}}\otimes{\mathbb{L}}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
(or
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{*\vee}_{i_{1}}\otimes\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{i_{1}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
after gluing) defined away from the zero locus
Z(\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}})
, and
Z(\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}})
behaves like its ‘poles’ (see Remark
3.3
). Then
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}\otimes{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
is a multisection of
{\mathbb{L}}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
(or
\ \widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{i_{1}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
after gluing) defined away from
Z(\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}})
.

Parts of zeros of
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
coincide with ‘poles’ of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}
: we have
\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{1}_{i_{1},nsp}\subseteq\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}
. For any
\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{1}_{i_{1},nsp}
, we denote by
\bm{H}:=d_{e_{\bm{G}}}\bm{G}\in\text{sGPI}^{r,0}_{1,B,I}
, by
r_{1}=i^{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}_{nsp}
and
r_{2}
the two roots in
RT(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})=RT(d_{e_{\bm{G}}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})=RT(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}})
. The restriction of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}\otimes{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
to
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{H}}
has two branches with weight
1/2
. One of them is pulled back from

\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}\otimes\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}},r_{2}},

(5.4)

the other one is pulled back from

\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}\otimes\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}},r_{1}}.

(5.5)

Notice that by construction we have

\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}},r_{2}}=\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\operatorname{for}_{r_{1}}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}})\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}\otimes\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}\to\operatorname{for}_{r_{1}}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}})}\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\operatorname{for}_{r_{1}}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}),r_{2}},

and

\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}\otimes\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\operatorname{for}_{r_{1}}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}})\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}=\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}\to\operatorname{for}_{r_{1}}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}})}\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\operatorname{for}_{r_{1}}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}})\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}},

therefore (
5.4
) can extends to a multisection

\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}\to\operatorname{for}_{r_{1}}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}})}(\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\operatorname{for}_{r_{1}}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}})\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}\otimes\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\operatorname{for}_{r_{1}}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}),r_{2}})

(5.6)

which has no zeros or ‘poles’ along
\mathcal{PM}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}
. So the zeros and ‘poles’ of corresponding branch of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}\otimes{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
along
\mathcal{PM}_{\bm{G}}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{H}}
cancel out: we can extend it as pullback of (
5.6
), which vanishes no where on
\mathcal{PM}_{\bm{G}}
. Meanwhile, the other branch corresponding to (
5.5
) has ‘poles’ along
\mathcal{PM}_{\bm{G}}
since
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}
has ‘poles’ and
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
while does not vanish.

After the above natural extension,
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}\otimes{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
, as a multisection of
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
(or
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
after gluing), has zeros along

\bigcup_{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},g=1}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}\cup\bigcup_{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},sp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}\bigcup_{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}\setminus\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{1}_{i_{1},nsp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}\bigcup_{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2+1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}

and ’poles’ along

\bigcup_{\mathbf{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{F}}_{i_{1},sp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G}}\cup\bigcup_{\mathbf{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{1}_{i_{1},nsp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G}}\cup\bigcup_{\mathbf{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{2}_{i_{1},nsp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathbf{G}}.

Step 3.
The following observation is useful not only in removing the ‘poles’ of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}\otimes{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
, but also in the computation in Step 5 below.

Observation 5.3
.

For any
\bm{G}\in\left(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},g=1}\sqcup\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},sp}\sqcup\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}\sqcup\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2+1/2}_{i_{1},nsp})\right)\setminus(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},co}\cup\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{1}_{i_{1},nsp})
, or any
\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{2}_{i_{1},nsp}
, the restriction of the multisection
\bm{s}^{re}
(see (
5.2
)) vanishes nowhere on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
.

To see this, we write
\bm{H}=d_{e_{\bm{G}}}\bm{G}
and
\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}=d_{e_{\bm{G}}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
. Since
\bm{s}
is
\gamma
-canonical, the restriction of
\bm{s}^{re}
on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{H}}
is of the form

\pi^{*}_{\bm{H}\to\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}\oplus\bigoplus_{\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})\setminus\{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\}}\pi^{*}_{\bm{H}\to\Gamma}\bm{s}_{\Gamma}.

By transversality, for
\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})\setminus\{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\}
, the multisection
\bm{s}_{\Gamma}
vanishes nowhere unless
\rk E_{\Gamma}\leq\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma}
, so we can focus on the case
\rk E_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}\geq\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}
.

We denote by
\bar{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\in\partial^{*}\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}
the graph obtained after stabilizing
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
(
i.e.
contract every unstable vertices
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
); it is stable since it has
i_{1}
and at least one internal root as tails. Because of transversely-decomposability, the restriction of
\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}
on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}
is pulled back from a transverse multisection of
E^{re}_{\mathcal{T}_{S}\bar{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{T}_{S}\bar{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
(see §
4.1
), where
S=RT(\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}})\cap T^{I}({\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}})
. We always have

\rk E^{re}_{\mathcal{T}_{S}\bar{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}=\rk E^{re}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}=\rk E_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}-2,

and

\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{T}_{S}\bar{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\leq\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bar{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\leq\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}-2,

but the first inequality is strict for
\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{2}_{i_{1},nsp}
, while the second inequality is strict for
\bm{G}\in\left(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},g=1}\sqcup\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},sp}\sqcup\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2}_{i_{1},nsp}\sqcup\bm{\mathcal{G}}^{1/2+1/2}_{i_{1},nsp})\right)\setminus(\bm{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{1},co}\cup\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{1}_{i_{1},nsp})
, therefore for all the
\bm{G}
in these sets we have
\rk E^{re}_{\mathcal{T}_{S}\bar{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{T}_{S}\bar{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
, hence
\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}
vanishes nowhere on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
.

According to the above observation, we can take an open neighbourhood
U^{2}_{nsp}
of

Z^{2}_{nsp}:=\bigcup_{\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{F}}^{2}_{i_{1},nsp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}

which glue to an open set in
\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
, such that
\bm{s}^{re}
vanishes nowhere in
U^{2}_{nsp}
.

For fixed
R\subseteq I\setminus\{i_{1}\}
and
T\subseteq B
, the subspace

Z_{sp}^{R,T}:=\bigsqcup_{\bm{G}\in\mathcal{F}^{R,T}_{i_{1},sp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{1,B,I}^{1/r,0}

parametrize all the
(r,0)
-cylinders with unpaired markings
(B,I)
which have a separating dashed line connecting a twist-zero internal point
i_{sp}^{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\in I^{PI}(\bm{G})
and twist-
(r-2)
boundary point
b^{R,T}_{sp}\in B^{PI}(\bm{G})
, where
i_{sp}^{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
together with all unpaired markings in
R
and
T
are on the genus-zero part, and
i_{sp}^{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
lies one a closed irreducible component with only three special points: the marking
i_{1}
, the half-node
n_{\bm{G}}
, and
i_{sp}^{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
. If we denote by
a^{R,T}_{sp}
the other half-node of
n_{\bm{G}}
(it has the same twist as
i_{1}
), then after detaching the edge
e_{\bm{G}}
, except the closed
r
-spin sphere with three markings in
\{i_{1},n_{\bm{G}},i_{sp}^{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\}
, from the above data we get an
(r,0)
-disk with unpaired marking
(T,R\sqcup\{a^{R,T}_{sp}\})
together with an
(r,0)
-cylinder with unpaired marking
(B\sqcup\{a^{R,T}_{sp}\setminus T\},I\setminus(R\sqcup\{i_{1}\}))
, thus we have an detach morphism

\operatorname{Detach}_{sp}^{R,T}\colon Z^{R,T}_{sp}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B\sqcup\{b^{R,T}_{sp}\}\setminus T,I\setminus(R\sqcup\{i_{1}\})}\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{0,T,R\sqcup\{a^{R,T}_{sp}\}}\times{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{v_{sp,c}^{R,T}},

(5.7)

where
v_{sp,c}^{R,T}
is a closed single-vertex
r
-spin graph with three tails
i_{1},n_{\bm{G}}
and
i_{sp}^{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
. Note that
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{v_{sp,c}^{R,T}}
is a zero-dimensional orbifold with an order-
r
isotopy group and rank-zero Witten bundle.
Moreover, the point insertion procedure glues
Z^{R,T}_{sp}
to a subspace
\widetilde{Z}^{R,T}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
, the detach morphisms
\operatorname{Detach}_{e_{\bm{G}}}
also glue to a morphism

\operatorname{Detach}_{sp}^{R,T}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B\sqcup\{b^{R,T}_{sp}\}\setminus T,I\setminus(R\sqcup\{i_{1}\})}\times\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{0,T,R\sqcup\{a^{R,T}_{sp}\}}\times{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{v_{sp,c}^{R,T}}.

Similar, the subspace

Z_{nsp}^{1}:=\bigsqcup_{\bm{G}\in\mathcal{F}^{1}_{i_{1},nsp}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{1,B,I}^{1/r,0}

also has a detach morphism

\operatorname{Detach}_{nsp}\colon Z_{nsp}^{1}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B\sqcup\{b_{nsp}\},I\sqcup\{a_{nsp}\}\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\times{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{v_{nsp,c}},

(5.8)

where
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{v_{nsp,c}}
is a zero-dimensional orbifold with an order-
r
isotopy group and rank-zero Witten bundle; the new twist-
(r-2)
boundary marking
b_{nsp}
was the one paired with
i_{nsp}^{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
, and the new internal marking
a_{nsp}
was the other half-node of
n_{\bm{G}}
, it has the same twist as
i_{1}
. Under point insertion,
\operatorname{Detach}_{nsp}
also glues to

\operatorname{Detach}_{nsp}\colon\widetilde{Z}^{1}_{nsp}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{0,B\sqcup\{b_{nsp}\},I\sqcup\{a_{nsp}\}\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\times{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{v_{nsp,c}}.

The locus
\widetilde{Z}^{R,T}_{sp}
is the ’pole’ of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}\otimes{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
because it is the ‘pole’ of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}
. Note that
\widetilde{Z}^{R,T}_{sp}
does not intersect with any other ‘poles’, by the method in Remark
3.3
, we can modify
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}
in a small neighbourhood
U^{R,T}_{sp}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
of
\widetilde{Z}^{R,T}_{sp}
to a section with zero at
\widetilde{Z}^{R,T}_{sp}
, but with the reversed order of vanishing as
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}
.

Similarly, the locus
\widetilde{Z}^{1}_{nsp}
is the ’pole’ of one branch of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}\otimes{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
because it is the ‘pole’ of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}
, and in this branch
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
does not vanish (recall that in the other branch the zero of
{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
canceled with the ‘pole’ of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}
).
Note that
\widetilde{Z}^{1}_{nsp}
does not intersect with any other ‘poles’ outside the neighbourhood
U^{2}_{nsp}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
, by the method in Remark
3.3
, we can find a small neighbourhood
U^{1}_{nsp}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
of
\widetilde{Z}^{1}_{nsp}
and modify the branch of
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}\otimes{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1}}
with ‘poles’ by modifying
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}
in this branch, in
U^{1}_{nsp}\setminus U^{2}_{nsp}
, to a section with zero at
\widetilde{Z}^{R,T}_{sp}\setminus U^{2}_{nsp}
, but with the reversed order of vanishing as
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*}}
.

After these modifications, we get a multisection of
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}
on
\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}\setminus U^{2}_{nsp}
. We can then extend it to an arbitrary multisection of
\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}
over the entire
\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
. We denote such an extension by
s_{i_{1}}^{trr}
.
As the modifications and extensions can all be done first on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}
, and then pulled back to
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{H}}
, we require, for every
\bm{H}\in\text{sGPI}_{1,B,I}^{r,0}
, that

s_{i_{1},\bm{H}}^{trr}:=s_{i_{1}}^{trr}|_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{H}}}=\left(\operatorname{For}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}\circ\pi_{\bm{H}\to\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}\right)^{*}s_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}^{trr},

where
s
is a multisection of
\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}\to{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}
, which is a modification
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}\otimes\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{H}}}
near its “pole”.
This requirement is fulfilled via an induction process very similar to §
6.2
, and we omit the details.

Step 4.

Now we show that
\bm{s}
has the same number of zeros as
\bm{s}^{mod}:=\bm{s}^{re}\oplus{s}^{trr}_{i_{1}}
in
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
by constructing a homotopy between
\bm{s}
and
\bm{s}^{mod}
.

We denote by
\bm{\mathcal{H}}\subseteq\text{sGPI}^{r,0}_{1,B,I}
the subset consisting of smooth
(r,0)
-graphs
\bm{G}
with a genus-one vertex
\Gamma_{g=1,\mathbf{G}}
.
Notice that for any
\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}
, if
\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})
is genus-zero, then
\lvert RT(\Gamma)\rvert=1
.
We also denote by
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
the subset such that
\Gamma_{g=1,\mathbf{G}}=\Gamma_{i_{1},\mathbf{G}}
, and set
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{0}:=\bm{\mathcal{H}}\setminus\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
.

Observation 5.4
.

Note that for any
\bm{G}\in\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
,
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
does not intersect with
Z^{R,T}_{sp}
,
Z^{1}_{nsp}
or
Z^{2}_{nsp}
, so in this case the restriction
s_{i_{i},\bm{G}}^{trr}
of
s_{i_{i}}^{trr}
to
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
is just
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}^{\vee}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*},\bm{G}}\otimes{s}^{trr*}_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
since we took
U^{R,T}_{sp}
,
U^{1}_{nsp}
and
U^{2}_{nsp}
small enough.
Moreover, since
\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
is genus-one, we have
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}=\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
, therefore
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
, and hence
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{i_{1},\mathbb{L}\to\mathbb{L}^{*},\bm{G}}
, are just constant identity sections of trivial complex line bundles, so we have
s_{i_{i},\bm{G}}^{trr}=s_{i_{i},\bm{G}}^{trr*}
and both of them are the pullback of
\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
.
On the other hand, since
\bm{s}
is
\gamma
-canonical, by definition the restriction
t_{D,\bm{G}}
of
t_{D}
to
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}
is pulled back from a multisection
t_{D,\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
via
\pi_{\bm{G}\to\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
and
\operatorname{For}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
,
and
t_{D,\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
coincide with
\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
in
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\setminus K_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
, where
K_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
is a neighbourhood of the zero locus of
\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
.
Notice that, according to item
5
in Definition
4.6
,
the multisection
\bm{s}^{re}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
vanishes no where in
K_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\subseteq{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
as long as

\rk E_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\geq\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}.

Now we construct a homotopy
H
between
{s}^{trr}_{i_{1}}
and
t_{D}
as a multisection of

\pi^{*}_{\mathcal{M}}\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{r,0}_{1,B,I}\times[0,1];

where
\pi_{\mathcal{M}}
is the projection to the first component; we require
H
satisfy the following conditions.

1.

H
glue to a global multisection of
\pi^{*}_{\mathcal{M}}\mathbb{L}_{i_{1}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{PM}}^{r,0}_{1,B,I}\times[0,1]
.

2.

For each
\bm{G}\in\text{sGPI}^{r,0}_{1,B,I}
, the restriction
H_{\bm{G}}
of
H
to
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\bm{G}}\times[0,1]
is of the form

H_{\bm{G}}=(\pi_{\bm{G}\to\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\times\Id_{[0,1]})^{*}H_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}},

(5.9)

where
H_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
is a multisection of
\pi^{*}_{\mathcal{M}}\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\times[0,1]
.

3.

When
\rk E_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
, or
\rk E_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
in the case
\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
is genus-one, the homotopy
H_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
vanishes nowhere on
Z\left(\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\right)\times[0,1]\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\times[0,1]
,
where
Z\left(\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\right)\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}}
of is the zero locus of
\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
.

To construct such
H
, we first take
H^{\prime}
to be the linear homotopy between
s^{trr}_{i_{1}}
and
t_{D}
,
i.e.,

H^{\prime}(p,\lambda):=(1-\lambda)\cdot s^{trr}_{i_{1}}+\lambda\cdot t_{D}
for any
\lambda\in[0,1]
. Note that
H^{\prime}
satisfies the first two requirements. If
\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
is genus-one, the third requirement is also satisfied: by Observation
5.4
we have

Z\left(\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\right)\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\setminus\operatorname{For}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\left(K_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\right),

while for any
p\in{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\setminus\operatorname{For}^{-1}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\left(K_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\right)
and
\lambda\in[0,1]
we have

H^{\prime}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}(p,\lambda)=(1-\lambda)\cdot\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}(p)+\lambda\cdot t_{D,\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}(p)=\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}(p)\neq 0

as the zero locus of
\mathfrak{s}_{i_{1},\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
is contained in
K_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
.

When
\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
is genus-zero and
\rk E_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
, the homotopy
H^{\prime}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
might vanish on
Z\left(\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\right)\times[0,1]
; however, this can only happen when
\rk E_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}+2
, because otherwise we have
\rk E^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}=\rk E_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}-2>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
,
6
6

6

Since every boundary marking of
\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
is legal, by (

), (
2.14
) and (

) we have
\rk E_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\equiv\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\mod 2
.
the transversality of
\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
implies
Z\left(\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\right)=\emptyset
. So we only need to modify
H^{\prime}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
for genus-zero
\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}
with
\rk E^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}

, which coincides with

on

\bm{s}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
and the transversely-decomposability of
\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
guarantee

dose not vanish on the end points, while

\rk\left(\pi^{*}_{\mathcal{M}}\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}\to\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\mathbb{L}_{i_{1},\mathcal{B}\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\right)=2>1=\dim\left(Z\left(\bm{s}^{re}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}\right)\times[0,1]\right),

guarantee that we can perturb away from the end points to avoid vanishing.

The collection of these perturbed
H_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}

who do not need to be perturbed, determine a new homotopy
H
between

and

via (

). The first requirement is still satisfied because the perturbations are only made in the interior

\partial{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma_{i_{1},\bm{G}}}
.

H
, then

is a homotopy between

and
\bm{s}^{mod}:=\bm{s}^{re}\oplus{s}^{trr}_{i_{1}}
.
We can show that for each

In fact, for any

If

Since after gluing we have

Step 5.

\operatorname{Detach}_{co}^{R}\colon Z^{R}_{co}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{v_{co,c}^{R}}\times{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I\sqcup\{a_{co}^{R}\}\setminus(R\sqcup\{i_{1}\})},

We can compute

by summing (

by definition, therefore (
