Remark 5.5
.

In the genus-zero case, Theorem
4.17
can be proven directly using a similar argument as above.

5.2
Independent of choice for genus-one open
r
-spin correlators

Theorem 5.6
.

For any two orders
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
on the set
\{i\in I\colon d_{i}\geq 1\}
, and two choices of
\gamma_{1}
-canonical multisection
\bm{s}^{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
-canonical multisection
\bm{s}^{2}
, we have

\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma_{1},\bm{s}^{1}}_{1}=\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma_{2},\bm{s}^{2}}_{1}.

(5.18)

Theorem
5.6
(which we will prove in the end of this subsection) indicates the genus-one
r
-spin correlators with respect to
\gamma
and
\bm{s}
are in fact independent of any choice of order
\gamma
or
\gamma
-canonical multisection
\bm{s}
, therefore we can make the following definition.

Definition 5.7
.

The
genus-one
r
-spin invariants (or correlators)
are defined to be

\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{1}:=\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma,\bm{s}}_{1}

for any order
\gamma
on the set
\{i\in I\colon d_{i}\geq 1\}
and and choice of
\gamma
-canonical multisections
\bm{s}
.

Remark 5.8
.

Since every boundary twist
b_{i}
is
r-2
in this paper, we can simply write
\sigma
in stead of
\sigma^{b_{i}}
. Following the convention in
[
4
]
, (and by an abuse of notation,) the open correlators
\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{g}
is also be written as
\left\langle\prod_{i=1}^{\lvert I\rvert}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\sigma^{\lvert B\rvert}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{g}
in §
1
for simplicity.

As a corollary of Proposition
5.1
and Theorem
5.6
, we have the following Topological Recursion Relation for
g=1

r
-spin theory.

Theorem 5.9

(Topological Recursion Relation for
g=1

r
-spin theory)
.

For any
i_{1}\in I
we have a TRR with respect to
i_{1}
:

\begin{split}&\left\langle\tau_{d_{i_{1}}+1}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{1}\hskip-5.69046pt\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{i_{1}\}\\
-1\leq a\leq r-2\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{a}\tau_{d_{i_{1}}}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau_{d_{i}}^{a_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},\text{ext}}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{r-2-a}\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{1}\\
&+\hskip-2.84544pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{i_{1}\}\\
T_{1}\sqcup T_{2}=B\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau^{a_{i_{1}}}_{d_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in T_{1}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\sigma^{r-2}\prod_{i\in T_{2}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{1}\\
&+{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\sigma^{r-2}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}.\end{split}

(5.19)

Proof of Theorem
5.6
and Theorem
5.9
.

We prove the (
5.18
), and hence (
5.19
), by an induction on
D=\sum_{i\in I}d_{i}
.

The case
D=0
is trivially true: according to Remark
4.14
the correlators in this case are always zero.

In the case
D=1
, there is only one element
i_{1}\in I
with
d_{i_{1}}=1>0
. Note that in this case
I^{d>0}=\{i_{1}\}
is a single-element set, so the choice of the order
\gamma
is unique. We apply (
5.1
) to the correlator
\left\langle\tau_{1}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{0}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma,\bm{s}}_{1}
and express it as

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{i_{1}\}\\
-1\leq a\leq r-2\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{a}\tau_{0}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau_{0}^{a_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},\text{ext}}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{r-2-a}\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{0}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma|_{R_{2}},\bm{s}|_{R_{2}\sqcup\{i^{r-2-a}_{I}\},B}}_{1}\\
&+\hskip-2.84544pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{i_{1}\}\\
T_{1}\sqcup T_{2}=B\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau^{a_{i_{1}}}_{0}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{0}\prod_{i\in T_{1}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{0}\sigma^{r-2}\prod_{i\in T_{2}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma|_{R_{2}},\bm{s}|_{R_{2},B\sqcup\{i^{r-2}_{B}\}}}_{1}\\
&+{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{0}\sigma^{r-2}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}.\end{split}

(5.20)

By Remark
4.14
all the genus-one factors in (
5.20
) vanishes, the only term left is

{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\prod_{i\in I}\tau^{a_{i}}_{0}\sigma^{r-2}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}

which is independent of the choice of
\bm{s}
.

Assuming we have proven (
5.18
) and (
5.19
) in in all the cases where
\sum_{i}d_{i}\leq D_{0}
for a integer
D_{0}\geq 1
, we now prove (
5.18
) and (
5.19
) in the case
\sum_{i}d_{i}=D_{0}+1
. We take
i_{1}=\gamma_{1}(I)
and use (
5.1
) to the correlator

\left\langle\tau_{d_{i_{1}}}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma_{1},\bm{s}^{1}}_{1}
on the left-hand side of (
5.18
) as

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{i_{1}\}\\
-1\leq a\leq r-2\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{a}\tau_{d_{i_{1}}-1}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau_{d_{i}}^{a_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},\text{ext}}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{r-2-a}\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma_{1}|_{R_{2}},\bm{s}^{1}|_{R_{2}\sqcup\{i^{r-2-a}_{I}\},B}}_{1}\\
&+\hskip-2.84544pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{i_{1}\}\\
T_{1}\sqcup T_{2}=B\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau^{a_{i_{1}}}_{d_{i_{1}}-1}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in T_{1}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\sigma^{r-2}\prod_{i\in T_{2}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma_{1}|_{R_{2}},\bm{s}^{1}|_{R_{2},B\sqcup\{i^{r-2}_{B}\}}}_{1}\\
&+{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\tau_{d_{i_{1}}-1}^{a_{1}}\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\sigma^{r-2}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}.\end{split}

(5.21)

Note that every genus-one factor in (
5.21
) is independent of any choice of the order or the canonical multisection by inductive hypothesis as
\sum_{i\in R_{2}}d_{i}\leq D_{0}
, therefore we can rewrite (
5.21
) as

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{i_{1}\}\\
-1\leq a\leq r-2\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{a}\tau_{d_{i_{1}}-1}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau_{d_{i}}^{a_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},\text{ext}}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{r-2-a}\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{1}\\
&+\hskip-2.84544pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{i_{1}\}\\
T_{1}\sqcup T_{2}=B\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau^{a_{i_{1}}}_{d_{i_{1}}-1}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in T_{1}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\sigma^{r-2}\prod_{i\in T_{2}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{1}\\
&+{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\tau_{d_{i_{1}}-1}^{a_{1}}\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\sigma^{r-2}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}.\end{split}

(5.22)

This implies that the correlator

\left\langle\tau_{d_{i_{1}}}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma_{1},\bm{s}^{1}}_{1}
does not depend on the choice of
\bm{s}^{1}
. Note that for the moment it still depends on
\gamma_{1}
since
i_{1}
in (
5.22
) is chosen as
i_{1}=\gamma_{1}(I)
.

We can also take
i_{2}:=\gamma_{2}(I)
and express the correlator

\left\langle\tau_{d_{i_{2}}}^{a_{i_{2}}}\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{i_{2}\}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o,\gamma_{2},\bm{s}^{2}}_{1}
on the right-hand side of (
5.18
) using (
5.1
) as

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{i_{2}\}\\
-1\leq a\leq r-2\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{a}\tau_{d_{i_{2}}-1}^{a_{i_{2}}}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau_{d_{i}}^{a_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},\text{ext}}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau_{0}^{r-2-a}\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{1}\\
&+\hskip-2.84544pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{1}\sqcup R_{2}=I\setminus\{i_{2}\}\\
T_{1}\sqcup T_{2}=B\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\tau^{a_{i_{2}}}_{d_{i_{2}}-1}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\prod_{i\in T_{1}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0}\hskip-2.84544pt\left\langle\prod_{i\in R_{2}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\sigma^{r-2}\prod_{i\in T_{2}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{1}\\
&+{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle\tau^{a_{i_{2}}}_{d_{i_{2}}-1}\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{i_{2}\}}\tau^{a_{i}}_{d_{i}}\sigma^{r-2}\prod_{i\in B}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{r},o}_{0},\end{split}

(5.23)

which only depends on
i_{2}=\gamma_{2}(I)
. Therefore (
5.18
) is proven in the case
\gamma_{1}(I)=\gamma_{2}(I)
.

To conclude the proof, we need to show that (
5.22
) coincides with (
5.23
) even if
i_{1}=\gamma_{1}(I)\neq\gamma_{2}(I)=i_{2}
. This can be done by applying the Topological Recursion Relations (
2.28
), (
4.8
) and (
5.19
) to the closed extended, open genus-zero, and open genus-one correlators in (
5.22
) and (
5.23
), in the following way.

-

For the closed extended correlators
\left\langle\tau_{0}^{a}\tau_{d_{i_{1}}-1}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in R_{1}}\tau_{d_{i}}^{a_{i}}\right\rangle^{1/r,\text{ext}}_{0}
in (
5.22
), if
i_{2}\notin R_{1}
, we leave it unchanged; if
i_{2}\in R_{1}
, we apply (
2.28
) with respect to
(i_{2},\{i_{1},i_{I}^{a}\})
, where
i_{I}^{a}
is the internal tail corresponding to
\tau_{0}^{a}
in the correlator.

-

For the genus-zero open correlators of the form
\left\langle\tau_{d_{i_{1}}-1}^{a_{i_{1}}}\prod_{i\in I^{\prime}}\tau_{d_{i}}^{a_{i}}\prod_{i\in B^{\prime}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{1/r,o}_{0}
in (
5.22
), if
i_{2}\notin I^{\prime}
, we leave it unchanged; if
i_{2}\in I^{\prime}
, we apply (
4.8
) with respect to
(i_{2},i_{1})
.

-

For the genus-one open correlators of the form
\left\langle\prod_{i\in I^{\prime}}\tau_{d_{i}}^{a_{i}}\prod_{i\in B^{\prime}}\sigma^{b_{i}}\right\rangle^{1/r,o}_{1}
in (
5.22
), if
i_{2}\notin I^{\prime}
, we leave it unchanged; if
i_{2}\in I^{\prime}
, we apply (
5.19
) with respect to
i_{2}
. Note that (
5.19
) is already proven in this case by inductive hypothesis.

After substituting these correlators with the results after applying TRRs, we get a new expression for (
5.22
). Note that in this expression
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
are symmetry.

We can also apply TRRs to the correlators in (
5.23
) in the same way after exchanging the roles of
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
. In this way we get a new expression for (
5.23
), which is exactly the same as the new expression for (
5.22
). This proves (
5.18
).

∎

5.3
Proof of Theorems
1.5
and
1.6

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

It was proven in
[
7
, §3.4]
that the right hand side of (
1.14
) satisfies the open
g=1
TRR, Theorem
1.4
. The TRR clearly fixed all coefficients of
\phi_{1},
hence Theorem
1.5
follows from Theorem
1.4
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.6
.

It was proven in
[
7
, §3.3]
that the conjectural all genus potential given in Conjecture
1
satisfies the Open String and Dilaton equations. Since we have proven this conjecture for
g=1,
in Theorem
1.5
it follows that these equations indeed hold for the open
r
-spin cylinder theory.
∎

6
Proof of Proposition
4.10

In this section we construct a
\gamma
-canonical multisection (Definition
4.9
) of

E=\mathcal{W}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}

for any order
\gamma
on the set
I^{d>0}=\{i\in I\colon d_{i}\geq 1\}
. This will prove Proposition
4.10
in
g=1
cases. Note that the
g=0
cases are contained in Proposition
4.16
.

6.1
Construction of
\gamma
-tame perturbations of TRR on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r}_{1,B,I}

According to item
6
in Definition
4.9
, we need to construct a
\gamma|_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
-tame perturbation of TRR for each genus-one
\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})
,
\bm{G}\in\text{sGPI}_{1,B,I}^{r,0}
. In this subsection, we will construct such perturbations in the cases where
\bm{G}
has only one vertex
\Gamma
which is a smooth graded
r
-spin graph with internal and boundary markings
I
and
B
. In other words, we will construct a multisection of
\mathcal{W}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{1,B,I}^{1/r}
since each connected component of
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{1,B,I}^{1/r}
is just
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Gamma}
for one of such
\Gamma
.
Note that in these cases we have
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma=\Gamma
and
I^{d>0}\subseteq T^{I}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma)
, thus we can write
\gamma|_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
as
\gamma
for simplicity.

A multisection of
\mathcal{W}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{1,B,I}^{1/r}
is of the form

\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}\bigoplus_{j=1}^{d_{i}}s_{i}^{j}

where
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}
is a multisection of
\mathcal{W}
and each
s_{i}^{j}
is a multisection of
\mathbb{L}_{i}
.
We will first construct the
\mathcal{W}
-part
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}
, then construct the
\mathbb{L}
-part
s_{i}^{j}
one-by-one, in the order

s_{\gamma(1)}^{1},s_{\gamma(1)}^{2},\dots,s_{\gamma(1)}^{d_{\gamma(1)}},s_{\gamma(2)}^{1},\dots,s_{\gamma(2)}^{d_{\gamma(2)}},\dots,s_{\gamma(\lvert I^{d>0}\rvert)}^{1},\dots,s_{\gamma(\lvert I^{d>0}\rvert)}^{d_{\gamma(\lvert I^{d>0}\rvert)}},

where we regard
\gamma
as a bijection
\gamma\colon\{1,2,\dots,\lvert I^{d>0}\rvert\}\to I^{d>0}
.

Notation 6.1
.

We denote by
G_{1,B,I}^{*}
the set of graded
r
-spin graph
\Delta
without non-separating internal edge satisfying
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Delta}\subseteq{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r}_{1,B,I}
and
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Delta}\neq\emptyset
(hence
\Delta
has no positive boundary edges).
Notices that each genus-zero vertex of
\Delta\in G_{1,B,I}^{*}
has a natural rooted or 2-rooted structure (see Remark
2.2
),
we denote by
\mathcal{V}_{1,B,I}^{root}
the collection of vertices (as single-vertex graph) of all the graphs
\Delta\in G^{*}_{1,B,I}
after forgetting a (possibly empty) subset of twist-zero illegal half-edges which are not roots.
We also denote by
\mathcal{V}_{1,B,I}
the collection of vertices of all the graphs
\Delta\in G^{*}_{1,B,I}
after forgetting the rooted or 2-rooted structure for open genus-zero vertices, and a arbitrary subset of twist-zero illegal half-edges.

6.1.1
Construction of the
\mathcal{W}
-part of
\gamma
-tame perturbations of TRR

We first construct the
\mathcal{W}
-part
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}
satisfying the following requirements.

1.

For each graph
\Delta\in G_{1,B,I}^{*}
, we require that
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Delta}
, the restriction of
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}
to
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Delta}
, is of the form

\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Delta}=\bboxplus_{u\in V^{O}(\Delta)}\bm{s}_{u}^{\mathcal{W}}{{\,\maltese\,}}\bass_{u\in V^{C}(\Delta)}\bm{s}_{u}^{\mathcal{W}},

(6.1)

where for
u\in V^{C}(\Delta)
,
\bm{s}_{u}^{\mathcal{W}}
is a coherent multisection (see Section
2.2.4
) of
\mathcal{W}_{u}\to\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{u}
such that
\overline{\bm{s}_{u}^{\mathcal{W}}}
is transverse as a multisection of
\mathcal{W}_{u}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{u}
; for
u\in V^{O}(\Delta)
,
\bm{s}_{u}^{\mathcal{W}}
is a transverse multisection of
\mathcal{W}_{u}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
. Recall that, unless
u
is closed and has an anchor of twist
-1
, we have
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{u}={\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{u}
and
\overline{\bm{s}_{u}^{\mathcal{W}}}=\bm{s}_{u}^{\mathcal{W}}
is a transverse multisection of
\mathcal{W}_{u}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
.

2.

For
u\in V^{O}(\Delta)
we denote by
\mathcal{B}u
the (single-vertex) graph obtained by forgetting all the twist-zero illegal half-edges. The multisection is
\bm{s}_{u}^{\mathcal{W}}
pulled-back from a multisection section
\bm{s}_{\mathcal{B}u}^{\mathcal{W}}
of the bundle
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{B}u}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{B}u}
via the forgetful morphism
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{B}u}
.

3.

If
u\in V^{O}(\Delta)
is genus-zero, we further require that
\bm{s}_{\mathcal{B}u}^{\mathcal{W}}
is positive in the BCT sense.

We will construct the multisection
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{B}u}
(hence
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{u}
) for all
u\in\mathcal{V}_{1,B,I}
in a consistent way. We only do it when
\mathcal{B}u
is stable since otherwise
\mathcal{W}_{u}
is rank-zero.

We start from
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{B}u}
for (closed or open) genus-zero
u
. Notices such multisection are
special canonical
in BCT sense, they are constructed inductively in
[
4
, §6.2]
.

After constructing
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{B}u}
for genus-zero
u
, we now construct
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{B}u}
for genus-one open
u
. We induct on the dimension of
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{B}u}
. Assuming we have constructed
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{B}u}
for all open genus-zero
u
with
\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{B}u}\leq n-1
. When
\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{B}u}=n
, for all
\Delta\in\partial^{*}\mathcal{B}u
, the restricted multisections
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\Delta}:=\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{B}u}|_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Delta}}
are determined by (
6.1
), and such restricted multisections are consistent in the sense they can be glued into a multisection on
\bigcup_{\Delta\in\partial^{*}\mathcal{B}u}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Delta}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{B}u}
, thanks to the inductive procedure. We extend this glued multisection to a transverse multisection on entire
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\mathcal{B}u}
using Lemma
6.2
to obtain
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{B}u}
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
M
be a smooth orbifold and
E\to M
be a vector bundle. For smooth suborbifolds
S_{1},S_{2},\dots S_{k}\subset M
which intersect transversally, assuming
s
is a section of
E\to\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}S_{i}
such that the restriction
s|_{\bigcap_{j\in J}S_{j}}
is transverse for every subset
J\subseteq\{1,2,\dots,k\}
, then we can extend
s
to a transverse section over the entire
M
.

Proof.

Any extension of
s
to a sufficient small neighbourhood of
\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}S_{i}
is transverse. We then extend it to a global section on
M
, and perturb away from
\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}S_{j}
to make it transverse.
∎

6.1.2
Construction of the
\mathbb{L}
-part of
\gamma
-tame perturbations of TRR

Now we construct the multisections
s_{i}^{j}
one-by-one. To simplify the notation, as in Definition
4.6
, we refer to the multisections

s_{\gamma(1)}^{1},s_{\gamma(1)}^{2},\dots,s_{\gamma(1)}^{d_{\gamma(1)}},s_{\gamma(2)}^{1},\dots,s_{\gamma(2)}^{d_{\gamma(2)}},\dots,s_{\gamma(\lvert I^{d>0}\rvert)}^{1},\dots,s_{\gamma(\lvert I^{d>0}\rvert)}^{d_{\gamma(\lvert I^{d>0}\rvert)}}

as

t_{1},t_{2},\dots,t_{D},

where
D=\sum_{i\in I}d_{i}
, and we refer to
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}
as
t_{0}
. We also denote by

T_{s}:=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{s}t_{j}.

We define a map
\nu\colon\{1,2,\dots,D\}\to I^{d>0}
in the way that
t_{j}
is a multisection of
\mathbb{L}_{\nu(j)}
.

We construct multisections
t_{j}
satisfying the following requirements, which are stronger than the requirements in Definition
4.6
.

1.

For each graph
\Delta\in G_{1,B,I}^{*}
and
1\leq j\leq D
, we require that
t_{j,\Delta}
, the restriction of
t_{j}
to
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Delta}
, is of the form

t_{j,\Delta}=\pi^{*}_{\Delta\to u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}t_{j,u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}},

(6.2)

where
u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}
is the unique vertex of
\Delta
containing the internal marking
\nu(j)\in I
,
and
t_{j,u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}
is a multisection of
\mathbb{L}_{\nu(j)}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}
. Note that if we formally set
t_{j,u}=0
for vertices
u\in V(\Delta)
not containing
\nu(j)
, as a section of a rank-zero bundle, then we can write

t_{j,\Delta}=\bboxplus_{u\in V(\Delta)}t_{j,u}.

(6.3)

2.

If
u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}
is open, we further require that

t_{j,\nu(j)}=\operatorname{For}^{*}_{u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}t_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}

(6.4)

as multisections of
\mathbb{L}_{j,u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}\cong\operatorname{For}^{*}_{u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}\mathbb{L}_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}
(see Remark
3.1
),
where
\hat{\mathcal{B}}u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}
is the single-vertex graph obtained by forgetting all the twist-zero illegal boundary half-edges of
u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}
except the root(s), and
\operatorname{For}_{u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}
is the corresponding forgetful morphism.
Notice that
\hat{\mathcal{B}}u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}
is always stable because it has an internal marking
\nu(j)
, and if it is genus-zero it has at least a root.

3.

For all
\Delta\in G_{1,B,I}^{*}
, all
u\in V(\Delta)
and
0\leq j\leq D
, the multisection

{T_{j,u}}:=\overline{\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{u}}\oplus t_{1,u}\oplus t_{2,u}\oplus\dots\oplus t_{j,u}

of

E_{j,u}:=\mathcal{W}_{u}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in T^{I}(u)\cap\nu(\{1,2,\dots,j\})}\mathbb{L}_{i}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}

is transverse to zero.

4.

For all open vertex
u\in V(\Delta)
as above, if
\nu(j)\in T^{I}(u)
, there exists an open neighbourhood
K_{j,u}
of the zero locus (see Observation
3.4
)
\left(\bigcup_{\Lambda\in\mathcal{G}_{\nu(j)}(u)}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Lambda}\right)\cap{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
of
\mathfrak{s}_{\nu(j),u}
in
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
, such that for any
p\in{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}\setminus K_{j,u}
, we have
t_{j,u}(p)=\mathfrak{s}_{\nu(j),u}(p)
, where
\mathfrak{s}_{\nu(j),u}
is the TRR multisection of
\mathbb{L}_{\nu(j)}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
(with respect to the rooted or 2-rooted structure of
u
if
u
is genus-zero) defined in §
3.2
.

5.

If
\rk E_{s,u}>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
for an open vertex
u\in V(\Delta)
and
\nu(s)\in T^{I}(u)
, then
T_{s-1,u}
vanishes nowhere on
K_{s,u}\subseteq{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
.

6.

If
\rk E_{s,u}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
for an open genus-one vertex
u\in V(\Delta)
and
\nu(s)\in T^{I}(u)
, then
T_{s-1,u}
vanishes nowhere on
K_{s,u}\subseteq{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
; as a consequence,
T_{s,u}=T_{j-1,u}\oplus t_{j,u}
vanishes nowhere on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
in this case.

Remark 6.3
.

For any vertex
u\in\mathcal{V}^{root}_{1,B,I}
, if
\rk E_{s,u}>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
, then then
T_{s,u}
also vanishes nowhere on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
.
This is a corollary of the transversality in item
3
.

Notices that since we set
t_{0}=T_{0}=\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}
, we have
T_{0,u}=\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{u}
for genus-one open
u
, they already (trivially) satisfy
item
6
because for all open genus-one vertices
u
we have
\rk\mathcal{W}_{u}<\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
according to (
2.15
).

Assuming we have already constructed
t_{0}=\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}},t_{1},\dots,t_{j-1}
satisfying the requirements above, we now construct
t_{j}
.

Step 1.
We start from constructing
t_{j,u}
for closed vertices
u\in\mathcal{V}^{root}_{1,B,I}
. If the marking
\nu(j)\in I
is not attached to
u
, we formally take
t_{j,u}=0
as a section the rank-zero bundle. If
\nu(j)\in I\cap T^{I}(u)
, we construct
t_{j,u}
by an induction on
\dim{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{u}
, such that:

•

For any
\Delta\in\partial u
, (
6.2
) is satisfied.

•

The multisection
\overline{T_{j,u}}:=\overline{\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{u}}\oplus t_{1,u}\oplus t_{2,u}\oplus\dots\oplus t_{j,u}
is transverse to zero.

For dimension-zero
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{u}
, we take
t_{j,u}
to be any non-zero multisection.

Assuming we have constructed
t_{j,v}
for all closed
v\in\mathcal{V}^{root}_{1,B,I}
with
\dim{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{v}\leq n-1
, we now construct a multisection
t_{j,u}
for
u\in\mathcal{V}^{root}_{1,B,I}
with
\dim{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{u}=n
. For any
\Delta\in\partial u
, the restriction
t_{j,\Delta}
of
t_{j,u}
on
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Delta}
is already determined by (
6.2
) in a consistent way: for different
\Delta_{1}
and
\Delta_{2}
, we have
t_{j,\Delta_{1}}|_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Delta_{1}}\cap{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Delta_{2}}}=t_{j,\Delta_{2}}|_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Delta_{1}}\cap{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Delta_{2}}}
by construction. Therefore, the multisection
t_{j,u}
is already defined on
\bigcup_{\Delta\in\partial u}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Delta}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{u}
, then we extend it to a transverse multisection
t_{j,u}
on the entire
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{u}
using Lemma
6.2
.

Step 2.

Now we construct
t_{j,u}
and
K_{j,u}
for open vertices
u\in\mathcal{V}^{root}_{1,B,I}
. Again, if the marking
\nu(j)\in I
is not attached to
u
, we formally take
t_{j,u}=0
as a section the rank-zero bundle, this includes all the cases where
\hat{\mathcal{B}}u
is unstable. If
\nu(j)\in I\cap T^{I}(u)
(hence
\hat{\mathcal{B}}u
is stable), we construct
t_{j,u}
and
K_{j,u}
such that:

•

For any
\Delta\in\partial^{*}u
, (
6.2
) and (
6.4
) are satisfied.

•

The multisection
\overline{T_{j,u}}:=\overline{\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{u}}\oplus t_{1,u}\oplus t_{2,u}\oplus\dots\oplus t_{j,u}
is transverse to zero.

•

The open neighbourhood
K_{j,u}
of the zero locus
\left(\bigcup_{\Lambda\in\mathcal{G}_{\nu(j)}(u)}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Lambda}\right)\cap{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
of
\mathfrak{s}_{\nu(j),u}
in
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
satisfies the following conditions.

(a)

For any
p\in{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}\setminus K_{j,u}
, we have
t_{j,u}(p)=\mathfrak{s}_{\nu(j),u}(p)
.

(b)

K_{j,u}=\operatorname{For}^{-1}_{u\to\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}(K_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}u})
.

(c)

For any
\Delta\in\partial^{*}u
, if
\pi_{\Delta\to u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}(p)\in K_{j,u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}
7
7

7

More precisely, by
\pi_{\Delta\to u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}(p)\in K_{j,u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}
we mean
p
is in the image of
K_{j,u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}\times\prod_{v\in V(\Delta)\setminus\{u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}\}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v}
under the gluing morphism.
for some
p\in{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Delta}
, then
p\in{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Delta}\cap K_{j,u}\subseteq{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
, where
u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}
is the vertex of
\Delta
containing the marking
\nu(j)
, and we take
K_{j,u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}={\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}
if
u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}
is a closed vertex.

(d)

If
\rk E_{j,u}>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
, for any
p\in K_{j,u}
we have
T_{j-1,u}(p)\neq 0
.

(e)

For open genus-one
u
, if
\rk E_{j,u}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
, for any
p\in K_{j,u}
we also have
T_{j-1,u}(p)\neq 0
. As a consequence,
T_{s,u}=T_{j-1,u}\oplus t_{j,u}
vanishes nowhere on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
.

We construct
t_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
and
K_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
(hence also
t_{j,u}
and
K_{j,u}
) by an induction on
\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
. For dimension-zero
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
(this only happens when
u
is genus-zero),
we simply take
K_{j,u}=\emptyset
and therefore
t_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}=\mathfrak{s}_{\nu(j),\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
,
t_{j,u}=\mathfrak{s}_{\nu(j),u}
.

Assuming we have constructed
t_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}v}}
and
K_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}v}}
for all open (and closed, constructed in Step 1) vertices
v\in\mathcal{V}^{root}_{1,B,I}
with
\dim{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}v}\leq n-1
, we now construct
t_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
and
K_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
for open
u\in\mathcal{V}^{root}_{1,B,I}
with
\dim{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}=n
.

We first construct the set
K_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
(and hence
K_{j,u}
) satisfying items
(c)
,
(d)
, and, in the case
\rk E_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
for genus-one
u
, item
(e)
.
K_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
should contain the zero locus of
\mathfrak{s}_{\nu(j),\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
in
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
, as well as some extra parts determined by item
(c)
. We define the unions of such subspaces by

\tilde{K}_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}:=\left(\bigcup_{\Lambda\in\mathcal{G}_{\nu(j)}({\hat{\mathcal{B}}u})}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Lambda}\cap{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}\right)\cup\bigcup_{\Delta\in\partial^{*}{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}\{p\in{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Delta}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}\colon\pi_{\Delta\to u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}(p)\in K_{j,u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}\}.

In the case
{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
is genus-zero and
\rk E_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}\leq\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
, we can take
K_{j,u}
to be any open neighbourhood of
\tilde{K}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
in
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
. In the case
\rk E_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
(or
\rk E_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
for genus-one open
{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
), we need to take
K_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
satisfying item
(d)
or
(e)
. This can be done due to the following lemma.

Lemma 6.4
.

In the case
\rk E_{j,u}>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
for any open
u
, or
\rk E_{j,u}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
for genus-one open
u
, for any
p\in\tilde{K}_{j,u}
, we have

T_{j-1,u}(p)\neq 0.

Proof.

We focus on the case
\rk E_{j,u}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
with genus-one open
u
. The argument for the case
\rk E_{j,u}>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
for all open
u
is identical.

If
p\in{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Lambda}\cap{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
for a graph
\Lambda\in\mathcal{G}_{\nu(j)}(u)
, then
\Lambda
consists of an open genus-one vertex
v_{\Lambda,o}
and a closed genus-zero vertex
v_{\Lambda,c}
. We have

\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v_{\Lambda,o}}+\dim{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{v_{\Lambda,c}}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}-2,

and

\rk E_{j-1,v_{\Lambda,o}}+\rk E_{j-1,v_{\Lambda,c}}=\rk E_{j-1,u}=\rk E_{j,u}-2.

Then
\rk E_{j,u}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
implies we have either

\rk E_{j-1,v_{\Lambda,o}}\geq\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v_{\Lambda,o}}

which means
T_{j-1,v_{\Lambda,o}}\left(\pi_{\Lambda\to v_{\Lambda,o}}(p)\right)\neq 0
by inductive hypothesis (or transversality), or

\rk E_{j-1,v_{\Lambda,c}}>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v_{\Lambda,c}}

which means
T_{j-1,v_{\Lambda,c}}\left(\pi_{\Lambda\to v_{\Lambda,c}}(p)\right)\neq 0
by transversality. In any case we have
T_{j-1,u}\left(p\right)\neq 0
by (
6.1
), (
6.2
) and Lemma
2.20
.

If
p\in{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Delta}
for some
\Delta\in\partial^{*}u
and
\pi_{\Delta\to u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}(p)\in K_{j,u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}
, we have

\sum_{v\in\Delta}\dim{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{v}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}-\lvert E^{B}(\Delta)\rvert-2\lvert E^{I}(\Delta)\rvert<\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u},

and

\rk E_{j,u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}+\sum_{v\in V(\Delta)\setminus\{u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}\}}\rk E_{j-1,v}=\rk E_{j,u}.

Note that if
u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}
is closed, then we are in the situation where
\Delta\in\mathcal{G}_{\nu(j)}(u)
, which is covered by the previous case. Therefore we can assume
u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}
is open, then
\rk E_{j,u}=\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u}
implies we have either

\rk E_{j,u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}

which means
T_{j-1,u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}\left(\pi_{\Delta\to u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}}(p)\right)\neq 0
by inductive hypothesis, or there exists
v\in V(\Delta)\setminus\{u^{\Delta}_{\nu(j)}\}
such that

\rk E_{j-1,v}>\dim{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{v}

which means
T_{j-1,v}\left(\pi_{\Delta\to v}(p)\right)\neq 0
by transversality. In any case we have
T_{j-1,u}\left(p\right)\neq 0
by (
6.1
), (
6.2
) and Lemma
2.20
.
∎

Therefore, we can take
K_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
to be a small open neighbourhood of
\tilde{K}_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
where
T_{j-1,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}(p)
does not vanish.

Now we construct the multisection
t_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
(and hence
t_{j,u}
).
On each
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Delta}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
for
\Delta\in\partial^{*}{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
, we take
t_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
according to (
6.2
).
On
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}\setminus K_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
, we take
t_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
to be
\mathfrak{s}_{\nu(j),\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
. Such choices are consistent by the inductive construction of
t_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}v}}
and
K_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}v}
.
We need to extend such multisection to the entire
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
which is transverse to the zero locus of
T_{j-1,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
.
Notice that, by inductive construction,
t_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
is transverse to the zero locus of
T_{j-1,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
when restricted to
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Delta}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
, as well as when restricted to
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}\setminus K_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
(since
\mathfrak{s}_{\nu(j),\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
does not vanish outside
K_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
).
Therefore, for any extension
\tilde{t}_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
of
t_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
, there exists an open set
U
containing
\left({\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}\setminus K_{j,\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}\right)\cup\bigcup_{\Delta\in\partial^{*}{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Delta}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
, such that
\tilde{t}_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
is transverse to the zero locus of
T_{j-1,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
when restricted to
U
.
Then we can perturb
\tilde{t}_{j,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
by a section supported in
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}\setminus U
to make it transverse to the zero locus of
T_{j-1,{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}}
on the entire
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
.

6.2
Construction of a
\gamma
-canonical multisection on
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}

In this subsection, we construct a
\gamma
-canonical multisection of
E=\mathcal{W}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathbb{L}_{i}^{\oplus d_{i}}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}^{1/r,0}_{1,B,I}
using the
\gamma
-tame perturbations of TRR constructed in §
6.1
.

Under the notation in Definition
4.9
, in order to guarantee item
3
and
4
in Definition
4.9
, we need to construct a multisection
\tilde{\bm{s}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
of

E_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}=\mathcal{W}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I\cap T^{I}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma)}\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\to\mathcal{B}\Gamma}\mathbb{L}_{i,\mathcal{B}\Gamma}^{\oplus d_{i}}

for each
\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})
,
\bm{G}\in\text{sGPI}^{r,0}_{1,B,I}
in a transversely-decomposable way.

Remark 6.5
.

Note that every such (single-vertex)
r
-spin graph
\Gamma
can be obtained from a (not necessary unique) graph
\mathcal{P}\Gamma\in\mathcal{V}_{1,B,I}^{root}
(see Notation
6.1
) after turning every twist-zero illegal boundary tail of
\mathcal{P}\Gamma
into an inserted twist-zero internal tail (
i.e.,
point insertion procedure). The graph
\Gamma
and
\mathcal{P}\Gamma
coincide after forgetting these twist-zero tails.

Recall that (see §
2.4.3
)
\mathcal{B}\Gamma
is the graph forgetting all inserted internal tails in
I^{PI}(\bm{G})\cap T^{I}(\Gamma)
, and
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma
is the graph forgetting all inserted internal tails in
I^{PI}_{sp,1}(\bm{G})\cap T^{I}(\Gamma)
. Such notations are consistent with the notations in §
6.1.1
and §
6.1.2
(which forget the twist-zero illegal boundary tails) in the sense that all inserted internal tails in
I^{PI}(\bm{G})
come from twist-zero illegal tails, while
I^{PI}_{sp,1}(\bm{G})\subseteq I^{PI}(\bm{G})
correspond to twist-zero illegal boundary tails which are not roots.

Moreover, for any
\Lambda\in\partial^{*}\Gamma
, and any
u\in V(\Lambda)
, we denote by
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Lambda
or
\hat{\mathcal{B}}u
the graph obtained by forgetting all the internal tails in
I^{PI}_{sp,1}
and all the twist-zero illegal boundary tails which are not roots (
i.e.,
lies in
I^{PI}_{sp,1}
after point insertion) from
\Lambda
or
u
. Note that
\hat{\mathcal{B}}u
is always stable if
T^{I}(u)\cap I\neq\emptyset
.
We also denote by
\mathcal{B}\Lambda
or
\mathcal{B}u
the graph obtained by forgetting all the internal tails in
I^{PI}
and all the twist-zero illegal boundary tails from
\Lambda
or
u
.

Note that we can always regard
\mathcal{B}u
as element in
\mathcal{V}_{1,B,I}
. If
u
has no internal roots (when
\Gamma
is genus-one, we regard half-edges of
u
as roots if they are either in
RT(\Gamma)
, or connect
u
to a component containing tails in
RT(\Gamma)
), we can also regard
\hat{\mathcal{B}}u
as element in
\mathcal{V}_{1,B,I}^{root}
.

We decompose
\tilde{\bm{s}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}
into
\mathcal{W}
-part and
\mathbb{L}
-part as

\tilde{\bm{s}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}=\tilde{\bm{s}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}}\oplus\tilde{\bm{s}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}^{\mathbb{L}}
. In the case
\mathcal{B}u
is unstable, we don’t need to construct
\tilde{\bm{s}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}}
as
\mathcal{W}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
is rank-zero. If
\mathcal{B}u
is stable, then we simply take the
\mathcal{W}
-part
\tilde{\bm{s}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}^{\mathcal{W}}
to be

\hat{\bm{s}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}^{\mathcal{W}}:=\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u\to{\mathcal{B}}u}\bm{s}_{{\mathcal{B}}u}^{\mathcal{W}}

as a multisection of the Witten bundle
\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u\to{\mathcal{B}}u}\mathcal{W}_{{\mathcal{B}}u}\cong\mathcal{W}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}u}
, where
\bm{s}_{{\mathcal{B}}u}^{\mathcal{W}}
is the multisection of
\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{B}u}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{{\mathcal{B}}u}
constructed in §
6.1.1
after regarding
\mathcal{B}u
as element in
\mathcal{V}_{1,B,I}
as in Remark
6.5
. Such choices will guarantee item
5
in Definition
4.9
.

Then we need to construct the
\mathbb{L}
-part
\tilde{\bm{s}}_{\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma}^{\mathbb{L}}
for each
\Gamma
such that
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma
is stable (otherwise the bundle is rank-zero). In fact, we will construct a collection of multisection
\{\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathbb{L}}_{j,v}\}
of

\operatorname{For}^{*}_{v\to\mathcal{B}v}\mathbb{L}_{\nu(j),\mathcal{B}v}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v}

for each
v\in V(\mathcal{T}_{RT(\Gamma)\cap T^{I}(\Gamma)}\Delta)
,
\Delta\in\partial^{*}\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma
,
\Gamma\in V(\bm{G})
,
\bm{G}\in\text{sGPI}^{r,0}_{1,B,I}
,
1\leq j\leq D=\sum_{i\in I}d_{i}
,
\nu(j)\in T^{I}(v)
, where
\mathcal{T}_{RT(\Delta\cap T^{I}(\Delta))}\Delta
is the graph defined in §
4.1
when taking
S=RT(\Gamma)\cap T^{I}(\Gamma)
,
\mathcal{B}v
is the graph forgetting all the tails in
T^{I}(v)\cap I^{PI}(\bm{G})
and illegal twist-zero half-edges, and
\nu\colon\{1,2,\dots,D\}\to I^{d>0}
is the same map in §
6.1.2
. By construction in §
4.1
such
v
is always stable.

Notice that each genus-zero vertex
u\in V(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma)
has one or two half-edges as root(s): they are determined by the topological structure of the cylinder as in Remark
2.2
when
\Gamma
is genus-one, and determined by
RT(\Gamma)
in a similar way when
\Gamma
is genus-one. Moreover, there are also rooted or 2-rooted structures for
v\in V(\mathcal{T}_{S}\Delta)
: whenever a vertex with root(s) on it is contracted (see §
4.1
for the construction of
\mathcal{T}_{S}\Delta
via contracting unstable vertices), the nearby half-edges become new roots (after possible detaching).

The collection
\{\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathbb{L}}_{j,v}\}
will satisfy the following conditions.

1.

For any
1\leq s\leq D
, the multisection

\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq s\\
\nu(j)\in T^{I}(v)\end{subarray}}\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathbb{L}}_{j,v}

is transverse to zero when restricted to the zero loci of
\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\xi^{-1}(v)}
in
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\xi^{-1}(v)}\cong{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v}
(see Remark
4.2
) when
v
is open, and is transverse to zero when restricted to the zero loci of
\overline{\bm{s}^{\mathcal{W}}_{\xi^{-1}(v)}}
in
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{v}
when
v
is closed. This will guarantee the transversality requirement in item
2
and
4
in Definition
4.9
.

2.

If no internal tails of
v
(except the anchor when
v
is closed) are internal roots, we require that

\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathbb{L}}_{j,v}=t_{j,\xi^{-1}(v)},

(6.5)

for any
j
such that
\nu(j)\in T^{I}(v)
,where
t_{j,\xi^{-1}(v)}
is the multisections of
\mathbb{L}_{\nu(j),\xi^{-1}(v)}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\xi^{-1}(v)}\cong{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v}
constructed in §
6.1.2
after regarding
\xi^{-1}(v)
as an element in
\mathcal{V}_{1,B,I}^{root}
as in Remark
6.5
.
Note that in this case
\operatorname{For}^{*}_{v\to\mathcal{B}v}\mathbb{L}_{\nu(j),\mathcal{B}v}
coincide with
\mathbb{L}_{\nu(j),\xi^{-1}(v)}=\mathbb{L}_{\nu(j),v}
.
This requirement will guarantee item
6
in Definition
4.9
.

3.

For any
\Lambda\in\partial^{*}v
, we denote by
S^{\prime}=T^{I}(v)\cap RT(\Gamma)
the set of original internal roots of
\Gamma
on
v
(not including the new roots from contracting), we require that the restriction of the

\tilde{\bm{s}}_{j,v}^{\mathbb{L}}
to
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda}\subseteq{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v}
satisfies

\tilde{\bm{s}}_{j,\Lambda}^{\mathbb{L}}:=\tilde{\bm{s}}_{j,v}^{\mathbb{L}}|_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda}}=\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\Lambda\to\mathcal{T}_{S^{\prime}}\Lambda}\pi^{*}_{\mathcal{T}_{S^{\prime}}\Lambda\to w}\tilde{\bm{s}}_{j,w}^{\mathbb{L}}

(6.6)

as a multisection of

\operatorname{For}^{*}_{v\to\operatorname{for}_{S^{\prime}}(v)}\mathbb{L}_{\nu(j),\operatorname{for}_{S^{\prime}}(v)}\big|_{{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda}}\cong\operatorname{For}^{*}_{\Lambda\to\mathcal{T}_{S^{\prime}}\Lambda}\pi^{*}_{\mathcal{T}_{S^{\prime}}\Lambda\to w}\operatorname{For}^{*}_{w\to\mathcal{B}w}\mathbb{L}_{\nu(j),\mathcal{B}w}\to{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda},

where
w\in V(\mathcal{T}_{S^{\prime}}\Lambda)
is the unique vertex of
\mathcal{T}_{S^{\prime}}\Lambda
such that
\nu(j)\in T^{I}(w)
.
This will guarantee item
1
and
4
in Definition
4.9
.

Similar to the previous subsections, we construct
\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathbb{L}}_{j,v}
by an induction on
j
and
\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v}
.

Assuming we have constructed
\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathbb{L}}_{s,w}
for all
s<j
and any
w
, as well as
s=j
and all
w
with
\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{w}\leq n
, satisfying (
6.5
) and (
6.6
). Now we construct
\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathbb{L}}_{j,v}
for a
v
with
\dim{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v}=n+1
. Note that (
6.6
) have already determined the restriction of
\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathbb{L}}_{j,v}
to

\bigcup_{\Lambda\in\partial^{*}v}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v},

we need to extend such multisection to the entire
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v}
.

In the case where no internal tails of
v
(except the anchor when
v
is closed) are internal roots, we are forced to take
\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathbb{L}}_{j,v}
according to (
6.5
). Note that in this case, for all
w\in V(\mathcal{T}_{S^{\prime}}\Lambda)
appearing in the right-hand side of (
6.6
),
w
also has no internal roots (except the anchor when
w
is closed), which means
\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathbb{L}}_{j,w}
is also determined by (
6.5
). Therefore (
6.5
) is compatible with (
6.6
) in this case according to (
6.2
) and (
6.4
) as
S^{\prime}=\emptyset
. The transversality of
\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathbb{L}}_{j,v}
on the zero loci in this case is equivalent to the transversality of the multisections
T_{j,v}
or
\overline{T_{j,v}}
in §
6.1.2
.

In the case
v
has at least one internal root which is not the anchor, we denote by
Z\subseteq{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v}
the zero loci of
\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathcal{W}}_{v}
or
\overline{\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathcal{W}}_{v}}
direct summed with
\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq s\leq j-1\\
\nu(s)\in T^{I}(v)\end{subarray}}\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathbb{L}}_{s,v}
,
we first extend the restriction of
\tilde{\bm{s}}^{\mathbb{L}}_{j,v}
to
Z\cap\bigcup_{\Lambda\in\partial^{*}v}{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{\Lambda}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v}
(which is transverse by inductive hypothesis and the fact that
\operatorname{For}_{\Lambda\to\mathcal{T}_{S^{\prime}}\Lambda}
is a submersion) to a transverse multisection over
Z
using Lemma
6.2
, then further extend it to any smooth multisection over the entire
{\overline{\mathcal{PM}}}_{v}
.
